Chapter 1

The academic year 1986–87

A source-defined year, with two chronological layers. This chapter covers all
46 catalogue records 667–712 and the 128 supplied pages P001257–P001384. It fol-
lows manuscript order while retaining catalogue record numbers and dates; these
are identifiers, not a silently corrected chronology. The manuscript dates in the open-
ing spring sequence often say 1986 where the catalogue says 1987, and record 667
has an earlier handwritten year. Such conflicts are recorded at the affected talks.
Shared page ranges are not copied wholesale into both talks: headings, page layout,
and the mathematical continuity determine the attributed passage. In particular, the
Gurevich heading on P001303 qualifies record 684’s catalogue attribution, and the
Beilinson heading on P001319 recovers surviving material for record 694, whose cat-
alogue entry has no linked pages. A boundary fragment at P001257 is linked to its
earlier-year occurrence rather than reassigned to Kontsevich. The catalogue itself
was checked (Moscow Center for Continuous Mathematical Education, 2026).
The mathematical lines. String measures and determinants, arrangements of real
and complex subspaces, quantum groups and their representations, matrix integrals
and map enumeration, integrable systems, modular lattices, automorphic forms, and
cohomological constructions run through the packet. The apparently separate talks
often share a concrete obstruction: deciding what remains valid after a deformation,
identifying the correct quotient or completion, or distinguishing a formal generating
series from an analytic integral. The later fate is correspondingly heterogeneous. Sev-
eral talks can be matched to near-contemporary publications; others restate earlier
results; a few questions remain meaningful only after their missing hypotheses and
intended output are specified.
Inventory and assessment. There are 349 distinct indexed items, 21 problem-index
entries, and 129 corrections or provenance notices. An item is a mathematical unit,
not a page or a sentence.

        Class   Historical assessment                                       Items
          A     Published soon after by the speaker or Gelfand                 39
          B     Developed by others                                            24
          C     Partly developed                                               10
          D     No identified development after a documented search             0
          E     Known before the talk                                         242
          F     Not identifiable from the surviving notes                      34

Class records historical fate; it does not certify literal correctness. The separate fields
retain missing hypotheses, false literal formulas, partial publication matches, and the
actual level of source inspection. F includes unrecoverable fragments and unsup-
ported precise readings; it does not mean that their surrounding subject is unknown.


                                            1
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                     2

No item is assigned D: the searches did not justify an assertion that a precise idea had
received no development.
Bounded developments. Two class C questions support explicit work. For the quan-
tum KdV spectral problem, a complete level-two calculation exhibits a genuine Jordan
block in an irreducible, nonunitary Virasoro module. Thus an unrestricted eigenbasis
formulation needs an extra hypothesis; the notebook itself does not assert semisim-
plicity. For the two-source unitary integral, probability Haar normalization gives a
regular entire U (2) formula, an all-rank convergent moment expansion with a proved
tail bound, and an exact U (3) counterexample to a specified naive permutation-only
extension. These do not settle an all-grade spectrum or a generic large-N asymptotic
problem. Two further annotations rederive a known transverse-plane criterion and
certify the notebook’s existing answer of 96 cube regions. Independent derivations
or algorithms agree in every reported test; code and complete outputs accompany the
chapter.
Questions and continuity. The problem index separates construction of quantum
KdV charges from their spectral classification; finite multi-source integrals from the
broader reduced gauge integral; and established special cases from unrestricted
arithmetic conjectures. Earlier problem identifiers are reused only for exact recur-
rences, notably the determinant question GP-1982-40. Other earlier occurrences are
linked as antecedents where their quantifiers differ. Broad thematic resemblance is
not an identity. The toroidal reformulation of the Riemann hypothesis requires the
correct spectral condition, including temperedness; neither an unrestricted unitarity
slogan nor a related theorem settles RH.
Status and evidence. The cutoff is 12 November 2026. Newly circulated 2026 pa-
pers are explicitly identified as preprints, with the version and theorem actually in-
spected; their existence is not presented as independent proof certification. Every
bibliography entry carries a verification note, and unresolved leads remain distin-
guishable from results read in the primary source. The report provides counts by
speaker and area, records the difficult attributions and searches, and specifies how
a website should expose source, reconstruction, literature, and present status sepa-
rately.


1.1    M. L. Kontsevich, 1987-02-24: Formal uniformization
       of curves and the Virasoro action
record 667; source pages P001257-P001260.
   The mathematical contribution headed Kontsevich occupies P001258: a curve with
a marked point and formal coordinate is acted on by Laurent vector fields, and the
Virasoro extension lifts the action to determinant lines. P001257 is a continuation of
Beilinson, not part of this contribution. The catalogue date is retained, although the
heading visibly reads 24/II-85; neighboring headings consistently read 1986. Conse-
quently the record cannot establish priority relative to the paper received in December
1986.
1986-667.1     Infinitesimal uniformization by Laurent vector fields               Class A

  Kind; source. construction; P001258
  The notes. The notes define M  fg = (C, p, x), g = C((x))∂x , and γ as Laurent expansions
  of vector fields on C \ {p}; the stated action has stabilizer γ.
  Statement. For smooth connected projective complex curves with a marked point
  and a formal parameter, Laurent vector fields act infinitesimally on the correspond-
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                      3

 ing moduli stack. At (C, p, x), the tangent space is g/H 0 (C \ {p}, TC ), where the de-
 nominator is embedded by its Laurent expansion. This is a formal infinitesimal
 uniformization, not a claim that the coarse moduli space is globally a quotient by a
 finite-dimensional Lie group.
 Reconstruction. The stack/formal interpretation and smooth projective hypotheses
 make precise the shorthand “homogeneous space.”
 Fate. Kontsevich (1987, §1) gives this construction and its stabilizer. The paper
 was received on 3 December 1986 and published in 1987; this is the direct author
 match, independently of the notebook date discrepancy.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-667.2    Meromorphic lifts and the Kodaira–Spencer quotient                    Class A

 Kind; source. method; P001258
 The notes. A family is given a lift ∇t = ∂t + F (x, t)∂x . One line identifies the tangent
 space with g/k, but the following displayed Kodaira–Spencer formula uses vector
 fields extending off p.
 Statement. A meromorphic lift of a base tangent vector, allowed a pole along the
 section, gives a Laurent vector field modulo vector fields extending over the punc-
 tured curve. For the decorated space its quotient is g/γ. Quotienting also by coordi-
 nate changes gives the appropriate tangent description for the undecorated moduli
 problem; g/k alone omits the curve-dependent stabilizer.
 Reconstruction. The first quotient is emended using the next formula and the pub-
 lished construction; no change to the diplomatic transcription is proposed.
 Fate. The tangent identification in Kontsevich (1987, §1) agrees with the second
 notebook formula. The isolated g/k line is a faithful mathematical error, confirmed
 by the scan.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
 tion: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-667.3    Virasoro action on determinant lines                                  Class A

 Kind; source. theorem; P001258
 The notes. The central extension e g, called the Virasoro algebra, acts on the pullback
 of the determinant bundle dk from Mg .
 Statement. Let λk = det H 0 (C, KCk ) ⊗ det H 1 (C, KCk )−1 . Its pullback to curves with a
 formal coordinate admits the corresponding lift of the Laurent-vector-field action
 to a Virasoro central extension. The central character depends on the determinant
 and cocycle conventions; the notebook does not specify a numerical normalization.
 Reconstruction. The determinant of cohomology is explicitly defined here; the notes
 only name dk .
 Fate. This is the determinant-line application in Kontsevich (1987, §2), published
 in 1987. The brief notebook assertion should not be expanded into an unrecorded
 central-charge formula.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  4

1986-667.4 Beilinson boundary fragment: constancy and metric                   Class E
normalization
  Kind; source. remark; P001257; A. A. Beilinson (boundary fragment)
  The notes. The preceding Beilinson continuation says that holomorphic functions
  on Mp , p > 2, are constant, and concludes that the zero-curvature metric is unique
  and can be obtained from a holomorphic section.
  Statement. This fragment is the continuation already indexed as 1985-666.21. Even
  when global holomorphic functions on the relevant moduli space are constant, mul-
  tiplying a flat Hermitian norm by a positive constant preserves its curvature. The
  conclusion is uniqueness up to that constant after the appropriate global trivial-
  ization and monodromy conditions have been supplied; zero curvature alone sup-
  plies neither trivialization nor normalization. The Mumford form is a section of a
  determinant-line expression, not a scalar function on unmarked moduli.
  Reconstruction. The record binding is editorial provenance. No new problem is
  created for an already indexed continuation.
  Fate. The determinant-line interpretation is Beilinson and Manin (1986); the precise
  earlier occurrence is 1985-666.21, with the metric qualification also indexed as
  1985-666.14. It is included here solely because this is the first catalogue record
  containing P001257, not because Kontsevich spoke this fragment.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: publisher metadata checked; development: none; confidence:
  high


1.2    A. Yu. Morozov, 1987-02-24: Bosonic string measure,
       determinants, and the conformal anomaly
record 668; source pages P001258-P001263.
   The actual talk begins on P001259 and ends on P001261 before Beilinson. It re-
duces the critical bosonic-string functional integral formally to a moduli integral and
explains the determinants, zero modes, and holomorphic factorization underlying that
reduction. Gelfand asks about multiplicativity of regularized determinants. The head-
ing reads 24.2.86; the catalogue date is preserved with this disagreement recorded.
1986-668.1     The genus-g bosonic measure on moduli                            Class E
  Kind; source. construction; P001259
  The notes. The functional integral over metrics and maps to R26 is written as an
  integral over Mp of |dµp |2 /(det Im T )13 times a vertex-insertion factor.
  Statement. On the smooth genus-g moduli space, after fixing conformal gauge, the
  critical bosonic integrand has a holomorphic determinant-line factor paired with its
  conjugate and a period-matrix factor (det Im Ω)−13 . Punctures and vertex insertions
  alter the corresponding line bundles and factors. This describes the integrand; it
  does not define a countably additive functional measure or prove convergence at
  the Deligne–Mumford boundary.
  Reconstruction. The distinction between an integrand and a convergent amplitude
  is essential and is not made in the shorthand formula.
  Fate. Belavin and Knizhnik (1986, §§2–5) derive the determinant and holomorphic-
  factorization expression; Beilinson and Manin (1986) identifies the Mumford-form
  interpretation. Both are 1986 publications, hence precede the catalogue date. Rel-
  ative to the manuscript date they are contemporaneous.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                   5

  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.2    Vertex operators and on-shell insertions                           Class E

  Kind; source. construction; P001259
                                   R
  The notes. The scalar vertex is eikx , and a vector vertex is written with one deriva-
  tive of x.
  Statement. The free-boson exponential must be normal ordered. An integrated
  closed-string vertex must have conformal weights (1, 1); a boundary open-string ver-
  tex has weight 1. A single derivative polarization is the open-string vector vertex.
  Closed-string massless tensor vertices require both holomorphic and antiholomor-
  phic derivatives. Momentum and polarization obey the corresponding on-shell and
  transversality conditions.
  Reconstruction. Normal ordering, conformal weights, and the distinction between
  boundary and bulk vertices are supplied to make the formulas meaningful.
  Fate. The vertex and Green-function insertion calculation is part of Belavin and
  Knizhnik (1986, §2). The notebook does not keep open and closed sectors separate,
  so the vector formula cannot be read as a complete closed-string state.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.3    Gaussian matter integration and zero modes                         Class E

  Kind; source. method; P001259-P001260
  The notes. Integration over 26 scalar coordinates produces a power −13 of a scalar
  Laplacian determinant and a Green-function exponential.
  Statement. For a fixed compact worldsheet metric, the Gaussian integral over non-
                                                         ′
  constant modes of D real scalar fields contributes (det ∆0 )−D/2 . The prime removes
  the constant kernel. Integrating the constant modes imposes total target-space
  momentum zero, while the source-dependent term is the Gaussian quadratic form
  obtained from the inverse of ∆0 on mean-zero functions.
  Reconstruction. The kernel removal and the momentum-conservation condition are
  restored.
  Fate. Belavin and Knizhnik (1986, §2) explicitly separates these determinant and
  insertion contributions. At D = 26 the exponent is −13; omitting the prime would
  give a zero determinant.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.4 Cancellation of the conformal anomaly at dimension               Class E
26
  Kind; source. theorem; P001260
  The notes. The gauge-fixed expression includes the determinant on vector fields
  and is independent of the conformal factor in dimension 26.
  Statement. For the bosonic Polyakov system of D free scalar fields and the
  reparametrization bc ghost system of weights (2, −1), the total central charge is
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                   6

  D − 26. Its vanishing at D = 26 cancels the local Weyl anomaly in this system; it
  does not by itself remove tachyonic or degeneration divergences.
  Reconstruction. The ghost weights and the scope of the cancellation are explicit
  here.
  Fate. The critical-dimensional determinant cancellation is established in Belavin
  and Knizhnik (1986, §3). The restricted system and the distinction between local
  anomaly cancellation and convergence are the needed hypotheses.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.5     Gram determinants and determinant of cohomology                   Class E

  Kind; source. construction; P001260
  The notes. The matrices N (j) are scalar products of zero modes, and the ratio in-
  volving det ∆j , det N (j) , and det N (1−j) is used to express the measure.
  Statement. For a family of Dolbeault complexes on K j , the Gram determinants on
  H 0 (K j ) and its Serre-dual cohomology compensate for changes of zero-mode basis.
  Together with a zeta determinant of the nonzero spectrum they define a Hermitian
  norm on the determinant of cohomology. The expression is a line-bundle metric,
  rather than an invariant numerical determinant without a chosen frame.
  Reconstruction. The basis transformation and determinant-line interpretation are
  supplied.
  Fate. The zero-mode factors occur in Belavin and Knizhnik (1986, §3); the
  determinant-line framework is Quillen (1985). These precede the catalogue date.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.6 Beltrami differentials as local deformation                           Class E
coordinates
  Kind; source. method; P001260
  The notes. The complex structure is varied by Beltrami differentials, described as
  Ahlfors–Bers coordinates.
  Statement. For a compact Riemann surface of genus g ≥ 2, infinitesimal deforma-
  tions are H 1 (C, TC ), dual to holomorphic quadratic differentials. A Beltrami differ-
  ential modulo infinitesimal diffeomorphisms represents a tangent vector to Teich-
  müller space. Local holomorphic coordinates on that space descend to orbifold
  charts on moduli, not necessarily to an ordinary global coordinate system.
  Reconstruction. The genus restriction and the Teichmüller/moduli distinction are
  restored.
  Fate. Belavin and Knizhnik (1986, §3) uses this deformation description in the de-
  terminant calculation. The method is expository, not a new claim of Morozov.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                    7

1986-668.7 Curvature of determinant metrics and the coefficient                   Class E
6j squared minus 6j plus 1
  Kind; source. claim; P001260-P001261
  The notes. The Belavin–Knizhnik variation is written as a mixed derivative of a de-
  terminant ratio equal to cj = 6j 2 − 6j + 1.
  Statement. The meaningful local statement concerns the mixed second derivative of
  the logarithm of the determinant-line norm, with nonzero spectra used. It is a cur-
  vature two-form proportional to a universal two-form on moduli, and the coefficient
  for K j is proportional to 6j 2 − 6j + 1, with normalization fixed by the curvature con-
  vention. A second derivative of an unlogged ratio cannot equal a universal scalar
  independent of coordinates.
  Reconstruction. This is a correction of the mathematical shorthand, not a claim that
  the missing symbols were visible in the manuscript.
  Fate. Compare the variation formulas in Belavin and Knizhnik (1986, §3) and the
  determinant-line formulation of Quillen (1985). The scan confirms that the note-
  book formula itself suppresses the logarithm and differential-form factor.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
  tion: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-668.8     Multiplicativity of regularized elliptic determinants              Class B

  Kind; source. question; P001261; I. M. Gelfand
  The notes. Gelfand asks whether det(A1 A2 ) = det A1 det A2 for elliptic operators and
  says the orders should be arbitrary.
  Statement. For invertible admissible elliptic pseudodifferential operators A, B of
  positive orders on a closed manifold, with specified spectral cuts also admissible for
  AB, determine log detζ (AB) − log detζ A − log detζ B. In general this multiplicative
  anomaly is nonzero; determinant multiplicativity is not an unrestricted law.
  Reconstruction. The admissibility and invertibility hypotheses are added to obtain
  a well-defined zeta-determinant question; arbitrary-order operators require a dif-
  ferent determinant framework.
  Fate. This repeats GP-1982-40 (1982-587.7). Kontsevich and Vishik (1994, §1.1
  and the anomaly formulas), circulated in 1994, gives a systematic treatment, with
  residue formulas under additional hypotheses and extensions beyond the positive-
  order setting. The formulation requires spectral cuts, which the notes do not fix.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the unrestricted multiplicativity assertion is
  false and the multiplicative anomaly is an established part of elliptic determinant
  theory. The earlier problem identifier is retained.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high; problem
  GP-1982-40


1.3    A. A. Beilinson, 1987-03-03: Mixed Hodge structures,
       real splitting, and Green-function periods
record 669; source pages P001261-P001265.
   The contribution starts on P001262 and ends at the beginning of P001265. It
presents mixed Hodge structures in filtration and split forms, then interprets an ex-
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                       8

tension attached to a punctured curve with two relative marked points through Green
functions. The heading reads 3/III-86, a year earlier than the retained catalogue date.
1986-669.1     Mixed Hodge structures over the integers                              Class E

  Kind; source. definition; P001262-P001263
  The notes. The data are a finitely generated abelian group L, an increasing rational
  filtration W , and a decreasing complex filtration F , with a pure decomposition on
  each weight quotient.
  Statement. An integral mixed Hodge structure consists of a finitely generated
  abelian group L, a finite increasing filtration W on LQ , and a finite decreasing
                                    W
  filtration F on LC , such that Grm LQ with the induced filtration is pure of weight
         W       L
  m: Grm LC = p+q=m H p,q and H p,q = H q,p . Tensoring kills torsion, which is retained
  only in the underlying integral group.
  Fate. This is Deligne's definition, published in Deligne (1971, §2.3). It is exposition
  in this talk.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct; publication match: exact; verification: read
  the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-669.2     Real splitting and a lowering endomorphism                            Class E

  Kind; source. construction; P001263
                                                          L p,q
  The notes. The alternative description uses  L   L C =      L , conjugate symmetry, an
  endomorphism N with N = −N , N (L ) ⊂ a<p,b<q L , and F = eN Fsplit .
                                          p,q             a,b

  Statement. For a real mixed Hodge structure there is a canonical real-split mixed
  Hodge filtration and a unique real endomorphism δ of strictly negative bidegrees
  such that F = eiδ Fsplit . Thus the notebook's N is iδ. It is nilpotent by the strict bide-
  gree inequalities. It is not the logarithm of geometric monodromy merely because
  it is denoted N . Rationality is required of W ; conjugation alone gives only a real
  subspace.
  Reconstruction. The canonical-splitting interpretation identifies the unnamed low-
  ering operator. The notebook's apparent inference from real to rational is not valid.
  Fate. The real-splitting result is stated in Cattani and Kaplan (1989, Theorem 1.15),
  referring to the 1986 work of Cattani–Kaplan–Schmid. The scan confirms the anti-
  real condition and the strict double inequality.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-669.3 Purity for smooth complete varieties and the                              Class E
direction of the weight filtration
  Kind; source. claim; P001263
  The notes. Example 1 says that a smooth complex manifold C has pure H a of weight
  a, and writes Wa = H a , Wa+1 = 0.
  Statement. For a smooth projective complex variety (or compact Kähler manifold),
  H a carries a pure Hodge structure of weight a. With increasing weights the con-
  ditions are Wa−1 = 0 and Wa = H a . Smooth nonproper algebraic varieties gener-
  ally have mixed cohomology, and arbitrary complex manifolds need not carry the
  claimed structure.
  Reconstruction. The compact Kähler or smooth projective hypothesis and the sub-
  script a − 1 are editorial repairs.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                           9

 Fate. Deligne (1971, §§2.1 and 3.2) distinguishes the pure proper case from the
 mixed open case. Both the omitted compactness/projectivity hypothesis and the
 wrong filtration subscript are visible in the notebook.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
 tion: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-669.4 The mixed Hodge structure of a punctured curve                                Class E
with relative points
 Kind; source. construction; P001264
 The notes. The example is H 1 (C \ {x1 , x2 }, {y1 , y2 }), with three weight pieces, and is
 drawn as a curve punctured at two points and with two relative endpoints.
 Statement. Let C be a smooth connected projective complex curve of genus g, and
 let x1 , x2 , y1 , y2 be pairwise distinct. For H = H 1 (C \ {x1 , x2 }, {y1 , y2 }; Q), one has
    W                    W                      W
 Gr0 H ≃ Q(0), Gr1 H ≃ H 1 (C; Q), and Gr2 H ≃ Q(−1). Hence dim H = 2g + 2. The
 homological version has dual weights and must not be interchanged silently with
 this cohomological statement.
 Reconstruction. Connectedness and four distinct points are imposed; the cohomol-
 ogy/homology convention is fixed explicitly.
 Fate. This is the relative-curve instance of Deligne's mixed Hodge structures,
 Deligne (1971, §3.2). The notebook example is explicit; the displayed graded
 pieces make its intended cohomological convention clear.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-669.5     Relative de Rham representatives and residues                             Class E

 Kind; source. method; P001264-P001265
 The notes. Differentials on the punctured curve are taken modulo differentials of
 functions with equal values at y1 , y2 ; residues at x1 , x2 describe the upper weight
 quotient.
 Statement. The relative de Rham complex for (U, Y ), U = C \ {x1 , x2 }, Y = {y1 , y2 },
 computes the relative cohomology. The condition f (y1 ) = f (y2 ) removes the end-
 point difference of df . The residue theorem leaves one independent residue at the
 two punctures, giving the one-dimensional Q(−1) weight quotient; the endpoint dif-
 ference gives the weight-zero quotient.
 Reconstruction. The relative complex, rather than an unrestricted quotient of all
 smooth differentials, is intended.
 Fate. The de Rham and weight-filtration construction is covered by Deligne (1971,
 §3.2). The equal-endpoint condition is the distinctive relative feature recorded here.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                              10

1986-669.6      Green pairings as mixed-Hodge extension data                                 Class A
  Kind; source. claim; P001264-P001265
  The notes. The lowering operator for the relative-curve example is related to an
  alternating sum of Green functions G(xi , yj ).
  Statement. For disjoint degree-zero divisors D = x1 −x2 and E = y1 −y2 , a normalized
  Green kernel gives the real pairing G(D, E) = G(x1 , y1 ) − G(x1 , y2 ) − G(x2 , y1 ) + G(x2 , y2 ).
  This is the real height-type invariant of the corresponding biextension mixed Hodge
  structure. Its equality to a specified matrix coefficient of N requires choices of Tate
  generator, orientation, and Green normalization; those choices are not recoverable
  from the brief notes.
  Reconstruction. The divisors and alternating pairing are unambiguous; no exact
  scalar multiplier is attributed to the speaker.
  Fate. The identification belongs to Beilinson's height-pairing theory, published in
  Beilinson (1987). This is a partial match: the precise normalization of the displayed
  notebook coefficient has not been verified in a primary formula.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the height/biextension interpretation is es-
  tablished, while the notebook's normalization remains an editorial uncertainty, not
  an open mathematical problem.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: indirect; development: none; confidence: medium


1.4    A. Yu. Morozov, 1987-03-03: String amplitudes,
       Green functions, and the superstring measure
record 670; source pages P001263-P001269.
   The continuation headed Morozov starts midway through P001265 and runs
through P001267. It connects the string description to gauge fields, discusses
explicit bosonic measures and theta functions, and asks how to obtain the superstring
measure by eliminating odd variables. The catalogue range includes neighboring
contributions; the items below use the actual headings. The manuscript belongs to
the 3/III-86 session.
1986-670.1      Ultraviolet motivation and numerical scale estimates                         Class F
  Kind; source. remark; P001265
  The notes. The notes contrast a string length near 10−33 cm with particle scales and
  describe other theories as inconsistent at short distances.
  Statement. This √ is a physical motivation, not a mathematical theorem. A string
  length is `s = α′ in a stated unit convention; its relation to a gravitational scale
  depends on coupling and compactification. No universal assertion that every non-
  string quantum field theory is ultraviolet inconsistent follows. The written proton
  size near 10−35 cm also conflicts with the scale comparison recorded on P001269.
  Fate. The critical-string framework is identifiable from Gross et al. (1985, Intro-
  duction), but that paper does not establish the universal assertion. The specific
  estimate is retained as notebook error rather than promoted to a research prob-
  lem.
  Present status. As of November 2026, this passage remains a historically identifi-
  able motivation with an erroneous numerical entry, not a definite unsolved mathe-
  matical claim.
  Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: thematic only;
  verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-670.2    Ward identities from integrated longitudinal vertices              Class E

  Kind; source. method; P001265-P001266
  The notes. A polarization proportional to momentum turns a vector insertion into
  a total derivative, and the massless string excitation is connected with Yang–Mills
  theory.
  Statement. For an on-shell open-string vector vertex on a worldsheet boundary,
  replacing the polarization by its momentum produces a boundary derivative of the
  exponential vertex. Decoupling in amplitudes requires the usual BRST or physical-
  state conditions and control of contact and boundary contributions. Chan–Paton
  labels supply nonabelian gauge factors. Closed-string massless tensor vertices give
  a different sector, including gravity.
  Reconstruction. The boundary, on-shell, and decoupling hypotheses delimit the
  total-derivative argument.
  Fate. The distinction between open-string Chan–Paton gauge symmetry and closed-
  string constructions is already explicit in Gross et al. (1985, Introduction); the BRST
  field-theoretic implementation appears in Witten (1986). Both precede the cata-
  logue date.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-670.5   Variations of regularized determinants                             Class B

 Kind; source. method; P001267
 The notes. The proposed calculation varies a determinant using the Green function
 at coincident points.
 Statement. For an admissible smooth family of invertible elliptic operators, the vari-
 ation of the logarithmic zeta determinant is a regularized trace of A−1 δA, with local
 correction terms determined by the regularization convention. The unregularized
 coincident Green kernel is singular and cannot be substituted into an ordinary trace.
 Zero modes require a reduced determinant and a separate kernel metric.
 Reconstruction. Admissibility, kernel treatment, and regularization are restored;
 the incomplete displayed theta expression is not certified.
 Fate. Kontsevich and Vishik (1994, §1) formulates determinant variations in a
 regularized-trace setting. The notebook's particular theta-function expression is
 too incomplete for an exact formula match.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: medium

1986-670.6   Eliminating odd moduli in the superstring measure                  Class B

 Kind; source. question; P001267
 The notes. The notes ask how to integrate over the odd variables of supermoduli
 and obtain an ordinary-moduli measure; the resulting modular ratio is expected to
 have weight eight.
 Statement. For a smooth super Riemann surface of fixed genus and spin parity, de-
 termine the pushforward of the RNS measure along odd moduli wherever a spec-
 ified projection exists, and its transformation and boundary behavior. A global
 holomorphic projection from supermoduli to reduced spin moduli is an additional
 geometric assertion, not an automatic meaning of Berezin integration. Local odd
 integration remains meaningful after a splitting is chosen.
 Reconstruction. The precise global-projection obstruction is a later formulation of
 one assumption behind the notebook's proposed calculation.
 Fate. Donagi and Witten (2015, Theorem 1.1), first circulated in April 2013, proves
 nonprojectedness in genus at least five. Version 1 of the preprint of Correa et al.
 (2026, Theorem 1.1), submitted 30 September 2026, states the nonvanishing of the
 primary obstruction on both parity components for every genus g ≥ 3, algebraically
 and holomorphically. The latter theorem statement was read; its proof has not been
 independently checked here.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, a global holomorphic projection is ob-
 structed in the stated genera, including genus three according to the September
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                               13

  2026 preprint. This does not assert that local RNS measures or physical amplitudes
  cease to exist.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high; problem
  GP-1986-01

1986-670.7    Determining higher-genus superstring measures                    Class C
  Kind; source. problem; P001267
  The notes. The computational question remains: find the superstring measure after
  the odd-variable calculation, rather than only its expected weight.
  Statement. In the genus-three even-spin case, on the nonhyperelliptic locus where
  a chosen pushforward is defined, determine the exact coefficients of the RNS top
  component in the three-function ansatz and prove the claimed normalization and
  degeneration behavior. This is a modern bounded subproblem of the much broader
  notebook request, not its verbatim formulation.
  Reconstruction. The three coefficient problem is a later precise specialization,
  clearly separated from the all-genus notebook request.
  Fate. Dunin-Barkowski et al. (2025, §1.7 and §4.9), circulated in May 2025 and pub-
  lished in October 2025, proves membership in a three-dimensional span but labels
  the coefficient values conjectural. Section 4.9.2 explicitly notes that the numeri-
  cal computations lack rigorous error bounds. The hyperelliptic polar behavior also
  prevents identification with an everywhere holomorphic ansatz.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the cited genus-three coefficient values
  remain conjectural in the opened publication; no claim of a complete higher-genus
  solution is made.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high; problem
  GP-1986-01
  Worth developing. Worth developing in a separate run: the three coefficient identi-
  ties and their normalization are concrete, but a complete RNS derivation and bound-
  ary analysis exceed the present bounded verification.

1986-670.8 Weight-eight forms and the genus-four Schottky                      Class E
relation
  Kind; source. theorem; P001267
  The notes. The notes say that cusp forms of weight eight first occur in genus four
  and identify a relation relevant to the string measure.
  Statement. For scalar Siegel modular forms of weight eight for the full symplectic
  modular group, the two rank-sixteen even unimodular theta series agree in gen-
  era at most three. Their genus-four difference is a multiple of the Schottky form
  and vanishes on the Jacobian locus. These full-group statements must not be trans-
  ferred to level subgroups or to spin-dependent modular forms, which have addi-
  tional forms.
  Reconstruction. The full modular group and Jacobian-locus qualifiers are added.
  Fate. Grushevsky and Salvati Manni (2011, §2) records these classical identities
  and their failure to force the analogous genus-five cosmological constant to vanish.
  Its preprint first appeared in September 2008; the identities it uses are older than
  the notebook.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1.5    A. Yu. Morozov, 1987-03-10: Compactification, lattice
       theta series, and heterotic gauge symmetry
record 671; source pages P001267-P001274.
    The contribution begins on P001268 and continues through P001273. It combines
gauge fields, the string oscillator spectrum, compactification on a sixteen-dimensional
torus, modular lattice sums, and root vertex operators. Several formulas mix ordinary
bosonic compactification with the chiral heterotic construction. The manuscript date
is 10/III-86, whereas the catalogue says 1987.
1986-671.1     Yang–Mills curvature and gauge transformations                         Class E

  Kind; source. definition; P001268
  The notes. The gauge potential Aaµ , its curvature, and infinitesimal transformations
  are written before the string discussion.
  Statement. For a Lie-algebra-valued connection one-form A, F = dA + A ∧ A, or Fµν =
  ∂µ Aν − ∂ν Aµ + [Aµ , Aν ], and an infinitesimal gauge transformation is δϵ A = d + [A, ].
  One must retain both antisymmetric curvature indices.
  Reconstruction. The matrix-valued convention avoids an unrecorded choice of signs
  for structure constants.
  Fate. The gauge-field conventions are standard background for the low-energy com-
  parison in Gross et al. (1985); they are not presented as a new theorem. The missing
  curvature index is a notebook notational slip.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:

1986-671.2     Massless vector states and allowed Chan–Paton                          Class E
groups
  Kind; source. claim; P001268-P001269
  The notes. The first excited string state is interpreted as a vector, and a simple
  nonabelian gauge group is introduced.
  Statement. The open bosonic string has a massless vector at oscillator number one
  in the critical theory. Nonabelian Chan–Paton factors in the usual oriented or un-
  oriented perturbative construction give unitary, orthogonal, or symplectic families
  subject to consistency conditions. A generic closed bosonic string on flat R26 in-
  stead has a massless tensor sector and does not produce this vector by the same
  construction. Arbitrary simple groups are not obtained merely by adding conven-
  tional endpoint labels.
  Reconstruction. The sector and gauge-group qualifications are necessary repairs.
  Fate. Gross et al. (1985, Introduction) explicitly motivates the heterotic construction
  by the limited Chan–Paton possibilities, including the exceptional-group issue. The
  notebook merges distinct constructions.

1986-671.6 Winding sums, Gaussian factors, and Poisson                            Class E
summation
  Kind; source. method; P001270-P001271
  The notes. After Gaussian integration the partition function is a double sum over
  Λ1 , Λ2 , with an exponential of a quadratic expression divided by Im τ . It is then
  factorized into theta functions.
  Statement. For a compact free boson, the classical Euclidean action is a positive
  multiple of kΛ1 − τ Λ2 k2 / Im τ , so the convergent winding factor has a negative real
  exponent. Poisson summation rewrites the winding sum as a momentum lattice
  sum. Holomorphic factorization into a specified finite collection of theta functions
  needs additional lattice and rationality data; it is not automatic for every real torus
  metric.
  Reconstruction. Positivity of the Euclidean action and the conditions for chiral fac-
  torization are restored.
  Fate. The compactification and modular lattice calculation is an essential step of
  Gross et al. (1985). The notebook suppresses sign and normalization conventions
  and also mixes a nonchiral absolute-square expression with a chiral lattice sum.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.

1986-671.9     Root vertex operators and the affine Lie algebra                  Class E

  Kind; source. construction; P001272-P001273
  The notes. Exponential operators indexed by lattice roots are said to have commu-
  tator given by the operator for the sum of two roots, and zero otherwise.
  Statement. For an even root lattice, the lattice vertex construction uses cocycles
  and a Heisenberg algebra. Root operators for α + β a root bracket to a signed root
  operator. If β = −α, their bracket produces Cartan and central terms rather than
  zero. The oscillator modes realize the corresponding level-one affine Lie algebra;
  these cocycle and opposite-root cases are indispensable.
  Reconstruction. The Heisenberg sector, cocycle, and opposite-root term restore the
  standard construction.
  Fate. This is the basic-representation construction identified with Frenkel and Kac
  (1980). Its publisher metadata was opened, but the full original construction was
  not read in this run; the exact identification is therefore marked indirect. The dis-
  played “zero otherwise” rule is false in the opposite-root case.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
  tion: indirect; development: none; confidence: high

1986-672.1 Intersection posets and complements of affine                           Class E
arrangements
  Kind; source. definition; P001274
  The notes. A finite collection of affine subspaces Li ⊂ Rn determines its complement
  and the partially ordered set of intersections.
  Statement.
  S             For a finite arrangement A of proper affine subspaces, put M (A) = Rn \
     A. Its intersection poset consists of nonempty intersections, including the empty
  intersection b 0 = Rn , ordered by reverse inclusion and labeled by dimension. Parallel
  affine subspaces can have empty intersection, so the resulting poset need not be
  the lattice of a central arrangement.
  Reconstruction. Reverse inclusion, the ambient element, and dimension labels are
  fixed to avoid sign and interval ambiguities.
  Fate. The convention is explicit in Greene and Zaslavsky (1983, §2) for hyperplanes
  and underlies the general arrangement treatment of Goresky and MacPherson
  (1988, Part III).
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.

1986-672.4 Order complexes, reduced degree minus one, and the                 Class E
small example
 Kind; source. construction; P001275
 The notes. Chains of a poset form a simplicial complex; an empty interval con-
 tributes in degree −1. The example is three coordinate planes and a generic line in
 R3 .
 Statement. The order complex has one simplex for each nonempty finite chain.
 Its augmented reduced homology convention is H     e −1 (∅; Z) = Z, whereas ordinary
 negative-degree cohomology of a pair is not being used. For the three coordinate
 planes together with a line through the origin transverse to each plane, the comple-
 ment has b0 = 8, b1 = 2, and no higher Betti numbers: the two chambers traversed
 by the line each lose an open radial ray.
 Reconstruction. Transversality means that the line is contained in none of the co-
 ordinate planes; reduced and ordinary conventions are separated.
 Fate. The empty-interval convention is required by the arrangement formula in
 Goresky and MacPherson (1988, Part III). The example is explicitly present in the
 notebook; its stated ranks are consistent with that formula.

1986-672.5   Which ordered sets occur as intersection data?                   Class F

 Kind; source. question; P001275; I. M. Gelfand
 The notes. Gelfand asks whether an arbitrary partially ordered set can occur.
 Statement. An intersection poset carries more data than an arbitrary order: an
 ambient element, intersection operations where nonempty, and codimensions con-
 strained by linear algebra. The notes do not specify whether affine or central ar-
 rangements, dimension labels, fixed ambient dimension, or fixed codimensions are
 required. These are different realization problems, and the question cannot be as-
 signed one exact classification theorem as written.
 Fate. The precise distinction between geometric lattices of hyperplanes and general
 intersection posets is documented in Greene and Zaslavsky (1983, §2) and Ziegler
 (1993, Introduction). The notebook formulation lacks the constraints necessary for
 a sharper match.
 Present status. As of November 2026, no definite new open problem is extracted
 from this unqualified realization question.
 Assessment. literal statement: not determinable; publication match: thematic only;

1986-672.6   Stratified Morse attachment and geometric splitting                Class E

 Kind; source. method; P001275-P001276
 The notes. The squared distance to a generic point is used as a Morse function. The
 notes explicitly warn that relative homology alone does not suffice: a geometric
 splitting must be checked.
 Statement. A generic distance function on an arrangement stratification changes
 sublevel topology at finitely many stratified critical values. Local Morse data de-
 termine relative homology, but a direct-sum decomposition of absolute homology
 needs vanishing of connecting maps and splitting of the resulting extensions. A
 generic long exact sequence of a pair does not supply this automatically.
 Reconstruction. The modern statement distinguishes local Morse data from the ad-
 ditional splitting argument.
 Fate. The method is developed in Goresky and MacPherson (1983) and Goresky

1986-672.7   Degree concentration for k-arrangements                            Class E

 Kind; source. theorem; P001276
 The notes. Each Li has codimension k, every nonempty intersection has codimension
 divisible by k, and a contribution of rank m is asserted to occur in degree m(k − 1),
 with rank |µ|.
 Statement. For a central real k-arrangement with the stated intersection condition,
 the intersection lattice is geometric with rank r(X) = codim X/k. Its open inter-
 vals have reduced homology concentrated in degree r(X) − 2, free of rank |µ(b  0, X)|.
 The arrangement formula therefore places the contribution of X in complement-
 cohomology degree r(X)(k−1). For k = 1, all contributions belong to reduced degree
 zero, together with the ordinary base H 0 .
 Reconstruction. The statement is restricted to central arrangements and its rank
 convention is stated. The notebook has a question mark over the equality.
 Fate. The specialization is part of Goresky and MacPherson (1988, Part III); Ziegler
 (1993, Introduction) verifies the geometric-lattice property and additive conclusion
 for k = 2. The question mark over the degree in the scan is resolved by the standard
 degree shift.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: indirect; development: none; confidence: high

1986-672.10     Finding useful cohomology generators and bases                   Class B

  Kind; source. question; P001276; I. M. Gelfand
  The notes. Gelfand asks how to find the правильный базис когомологий.
  Statement. For a complex hyperplane arrangement with an ordering of its hyper-

1.7    A. N. Varchenko, 1987-03-24: Asymptotic mixed
       Hodge structures and singularity spectra
record 673; source pages P001276-P001281.
   The actual contribution is P001277–P001280. It relates mixed Hodge structures
to powers and logarithms in singular integrals, develops the Milnor-fibre setting, and
describes the asymptotic filtration and semicontinuity of spectra. Gelfand asks for
several functions and for pairs of quadrics. These are separated from the established
one-function isolated-singularity theorem.
1986-673.1     Pure and mixed Hodge structures in families                     Class E

  Kind; source. definition; P001277
  The notes. Varchenko recalls pure Hodge decompositions, the weight and Hodge
  filtrations, and the holomorphic variation of the latter.
  Statement. For a smooth projective family, the cohomology local system underlies a
  variation of pure Hodge structure: the Hodge filtration is holomorphic and satisfies
  Griffiths transversality. For singular or nonproper algebraic varieties the appro-
  priate object is mixed. A locally constant underlying topological system does not
  make every weight or Hodge datum a topological invariant under arbitrary changes
  of algebraic structure.
  Reconstruction. The smooth projective and algebraic hypotheses distinguish the
  pure and mixed cases.
  Fate. The foundational structures precede the talk: Deligne (1971, §2). The varia-
  tion statement is background for Varchenko's asymptotic construction, not a claim
  first made here.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-673.3      Meromorphic continuation of powers of a function                        Class E

  Kind; source. theorem; P001277-P001278
  The notes. Integrals containing f λ are continued in λ, and rational powers and log-
  arithms are related to filtrations.
  Statement. For a real polynomial f , use a specified positive region and extend f λ by
  zero, or use a specified absolute-value distribution. Initially defined for sufficiently
  large real part of λ, it extends meromorphically as a distribution. A Bernstein func-
  tional equation P (λ)f λ+1 = b(λ)f λ gives continuation; resolution controls rational
  pole locations. A complex-valued f λ without a chosen branch is not a single-valued
  distribution.
  Reconstruction. The sign/branch convention is essential and is supplied.
  Fate. Bernstein (1972, Theorems 1 and 1') gives the algebraic continuation argu-
  ment (received June 1972, published in the 1972 volume). Its proof deliberately
  avoids resolution, while the stronger pole information comes from the resolution
  approach cited there.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established

formula
  Kind; source. theorem; P001278
  The notes. For f : (Cn , 0) → (C, 0) with an isolated critical point, the fibre is a bouquet

1986-673.5   Several functions and mixed Hodge structures                       Class B

 Kind; source. question; P001278
                                  R
 The notes. Gelfand asks about f1λ1 · · · fkλk and whether a map Cn → Ck gives some-
 thing like a mixed Hodge structure.
 Statement. For a specified algebraic map and algebraic fibres, their cohomology

 and admissibility or nearby-cycle hypotheses. Multivariate powers are governed

1986-673.7   Gelfand–Leray periods and logarithmic asymptotics                  Class E

 Kind; source. construction; P001278-P001279
 The notes. A holomorphic top form ω gives ω/df on a regular fibre, and periods along
 transported cycles have terms tα (log t)k .
 Statement. For an isolatedR hypersurface singularity and a chosen branch over a
 punctured disc, the period δ(t) ω/df has regular-singular asymptotic expansions with
 rational exponents and finitely bounded logarithmic powers. Monodromy eigenval-
 ues determine exponents modulo integers; a Jordan block of size s allows logarith-
 mic degree at most s − 1, not s. The exact convention for the exponent depends on
 using ω/df rather than another period normalization.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                 25

  Reconstruction. The branch, regular-singular condition, and block-size shift are
  supplied.
  Fate. This is the period setting of Varchenko (1982, §§1–2), whose Russian paper
  appeared in 1981 and English translation in 1982 after receipt in March 1980; the
  announcement Varchenko (1980) appeared in 1980.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.

1986-673.8    The asymptotic Hodge filtration and monodromy                      Class E
weights
  Kind; source. construction; P001279-P001280
  The notes. The Jordan form of monodromy determines a weight filtration, while
  leading asymptotic terms of forms determine a Hodge filtration.
  Statement. For the vanishing cohomology H n−1 of an isolated hypersurface singular-
  ity, the logarithm of the unipotent monodromy has its monodromy weight filtration
  centered at n − 1 on the nonunit-eigenvalue part and at n on the eigenvalue-one
  part. The asymptotic or limiting Hodge filtration contains information beyond the
  Jordan normal form. Leading exponents with their filtration multiplicities define
  the spectrum.
  Reconstruction. The two weight centers and the distinction between a monodromy
  filtration and the full mixed Hodge structure are restored.
  Fate. Varchenko (1982) develops the asymptotic construction; Steenbrink (1989,
  §1) states the Hodge-theoretic definition and relates it to the earlier isolated case.
  The notebook is reporting established work of the speaker.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.

1986-673.9 Bernstein roots, period exponents, and spectral                     Class E
numbers
  Kind; source. claim; P001279-P001280
  The notes. Exponents in the period asymptotics are related to roots of a Bernstein
  polynomial and to the Hodge spectrum.
  Statement. Bernstein–Sato roots, monodromy eigenvalues, and spectral numbers
  are related invariants with different normalizations and multiplicities. Monodromy
  identifies rational exponents only modulo integers. Spectral numbers also use the
  Hodge filtration; arbitrary exponents of every term in every asymptotic series are
  not simply the finite set of Bernstein roots. The precise equality proposed in the
  abbreviated line cannot be asserted without fixing the polynomial (including reduc-
  tion), signs, shifts, and choice of leading terms.
  Reconstruction. The finite leading invariant is separated from the full asymptotic
  series.
  Fate. Compare the functional equation in Bernstein (1972), the asymptotic filtration
  in Varchenko (1982), and the spectrum definition in Steenbrink (1989, §1). These
  sources support the relationship, not an unqualified equality of all three multisets.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established

1986-673.10      Semicontinuity of the singularity spectrum                      Class E
  Kind; source. theorem; P001280
  The notes. The final statement invokes semicontinuity of the spectrum under per-
  turbation.
  Statement. For a deformation of an isolated hypersurface singularity, the number of
  spectral values in a fixed half-open interval of length one, counted with multiplicity,
  cannot increase when the original singularity splits into nearby isolated singular-
  ities with the same critical value: the count for the central singularity is at least
  the sum of the nearby counts. The interval endpoints and common-critical-value
  hypothesis are part of the statement.
  Reconstruction. The interval and splitting hypotheses make the otherwise
  schematic final sentence precise.
  Fate. Varchenko announced semicontinuity results in Varchenko (1983); Steen-
  brink's 1985 result supplies the general theorem. The precise formulation was
  read in the author's later paper Steenbrink (1989, p. 166), which cites both works.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-674.2    PBW and the current-algebra degeneration                                Class A

 Kind; source. theorem; P001281-P001282
 The notes. The algebra has the size of a polynomial algebra and is a deformation of
 a matrix current algebra.
 Statement. For the normalized truncated Yangian over characteristic zero, ordered
 monomials in its nN 2 coefficients form a PBW basis. With coefficient degree q, the
 associated graded algebra is a polynomial algebra with weighted generators. With
 degree q − 1, its current-algebra degeneration has the truncated current bracket.
 These filtrations should not be confused with an assertion that all generators have
 the same degree in a normalized presentation.
 Reconstruction. The two filtrations and the normalization E = 1 replace the short-
 hand polynomial-size claim.
 Fate. Futorny and Ovsienko (2005, §5.2, Proposition 3) explicitly states the finite-
 level polynomial associated-graded theorem and credits Cherednik's 1989 paper.
 The two standard filtrations for the full Yangian are described in Molev et al. (1996,
 Theorem 1.22 and §1.26).
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-674.5   Yang–Baxter consistency and a PBW converse                        Class F

 Kind; source. claim; P001282
 The notes. The notes attribute a general connection between exchange relations,
 PBW, and the Yang–Baxter equation to Zamolodchikov, and mention trigonometric
 and elliptic alternatives.
 Statement. For a specified exchange algebra with an ordered reduction system,
 equality of the two reductions of a triple product leads to the Yang–Baxter equation.
 To deduce a PBW basis one must also control invertibility, admissible ordering, all
 ambiguities, and possible higher relations. An arbitrary quadratic quotient need not
 have PBW merely because a displayed operator satisfies Yang–Baxter; a universal
 converse needs a specified class of presentations.
 Reconstruction. The supported triple-overlap mechanism is separated from an un-
 supported universal equivalence.
 Fate. The rational RTT case is proved in Molev et al. (1996, Theorem 1.22); the
 Koszul deformation theorem (Braverman and Gaitsgory, 1996) shows why an extra
 structural hypothesis matters. No exact all-R biconditional corresponding to the
 unqualified sentence was identified.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the specific rational construction is estab-
 lished; the universal formulation remains under-specified in these notes.
 Assessment. literal statement: not determinable; publication match: thematic only;
 verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: medium

1.9     Yan I. Kogan, 1987-04-07: Covariant bosonic string
        states, constraints, and string fields
record 675; source pages P001281-P001287.
   The talk begins midway through P001282 and continues through P001286. It de-
rives the oscillator and Virasoro description, explains the mass tower and physical-
state constraints, and moves toward gauge symmetry and interacting string fields.
Several sign, mode-index, and level labels are faithfully wrong in the notebook and
are emended explicitly. The shared heading reads April 1986.
1986-675.1      Regge trajectories, tension, and the field-theory limit                  Class E

  Kind; source. remark; P001282-P001283
  The notes. The string slope controls an approximately linear relation of spin and
  squared mass, and the limit of small slope recovers a field theory.
  Statement. For the idealized string spectrum, the leading trajectory has J = α′ M 2 +a,
  with tension T = 1/(2πα′ ) in the standard convention. The limit α′ → 0 at fixed low
  external energies suppresses massive string states; the surviving effective theory
  depends on the open/closed sector and gauge labels. This is a controlled low-energy
  expansion, not equality of complete theories at finite slope.
  Reconstruction. The sector and low-energy regime are specified.
  Fate. The low-energy gauge and gravity interpretation is already part of Gross et al.
  (1985). The covariant bosonic construction in Witten (1986) realizes the interacting
  open-string setting discussed at the end of the talk.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  partial; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-675.4 The open-string mass spectrum and the level-one                       Class E
vector
  Kind; source. theorem; P001284
  The notes. The vacuum is tachyonic, the next state is massless, and higher oscillator
  levels form a massive tower.
  Statement. With intercept
                      P         one, the open critical bosonic string satisfies α′ M 2 =
  N − 1, where N = n>0 α−n · αn after the usual transverse reduction. Thus N = 0 is
  tachyonic and N = 1 is massless. A formula with mass itself proportional to N − 1,
  or without the square-root/sign convention, is not the same statement.
  Reconstruction. The notebook's mass normalization is replaced by the conventional
  squared-mass relation.
  Fate. The intercept and on-shell operator are explicit in Furuuchi and Ohta (2006,
  eq. (2)); the spectrum is the conventional Veneziano spectrum referred to by Witten

1986-675.6     Physical states, null states, and the no-ghost theorem              Class E

  Kind; source. theorem; P001284-P001285
  The notes. Physical states satisfy positive-mode constraints and the L0 equation;
  negative-norm states are removed in the critical dimension.
  Statement. At D = 26 and intercept one, the physical matter subspace satisfies
  Ln ψ = 0 for n > 0 and (L0 − 1)ψ = 0, then is quotiented by null spurious states.
  In the BRST formulation, one takes the relevant cohomology of a nilpotent BRST
  operator. The no-ghost theorem identifies the physical space with a nonnegative-
  norm transverse sector, under the usual momentum hypotheses; it does not say the
  unreduced covariant Fock space is positive.
  Reconstruction. The null quotient and the momentum qualification are stated ex-
  plicitly.
  Fate. The theorem originates with the early no-ghost work; the opened primary
  proof Furuuchi and Ohta (2006), first circulated in July 2006, gives the similarity-
  transformation argument and explicitly assumes nonzero light-cone momentum.
  That hypothesis is retained here.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-675.9 Associative string products and BRST string-field                   Class E
gauge symmetry
  Kind; source. construction; P001286
  The notes. The talk closes with string fields, BRST ideas, and a connection with
  noncommutative geometry.
  Statement. In the cubic open bosonic theory, the graded associative string prod-
  uct, a cyclic pairing, and a nilpotent BRST derivation permit an action of the form
  S = 12 hΨ, QΨi + 13 hΨ, Ψ ∗ Ψi, with gauge transformation δΨ = QΛ + Ψ ∗ Λ − Λ ∗ Ψ in
  the appropriate ghost degrees. Analytic definitions and normalization of the string
  product are additional structure; ordinary multiplication of component fields is not
  enough.
  Reconstruction. The standard cubic formulation is supplied as a contextual recon-
  struction, not an exact formula transcribed from this page.
  Fate. This identifies the framework of Witten (1986), published 5 May 1986. Only
  its institutional primary metadata and abstract were accessible here; the complete
  construction is not claimed as directly read. It predates the catalogue date but
  follows the manuscript April 1986 heading.

1.10     I. V. Cherednik, 1987-04-28: Quantum

record 676; source pages P001285-P001290.
    The actual talk occupies P001287–P001289. It repeats the finite-level RTT con-

theorem, and uses tensor products and Young diagrams to build representations. Ma-
trix size N and level n are repeatedly interchanged in the notebook. The date visibly
reads 28 April 1986.

  only the left side has L2 (−λ2 ).

  unintended relations and does not give the displayed coefficient algebra. The cor-

1986-676.2    Quantum determinant by rank-N antisymmetrization                          Class A

 Kind; source. construction; P001287
 The notes. The determinant is the scalar of the shifted tensor product L(λ + N − 1) ⊗
 · · · ⊗ L(λ), but the projector is labeled P− n.
                                                                                      V
 Statement. For N × N matrices in the RTT algebra, the top exterior power N CN is
 one-dimensional. With the compatible shift convention, its antisymmetrizer AN sat-
 isfies AN L1 (λ + N − 1) · · · LN (λ)AN = H(λ)AN . It is N , the matrix size, that determines
 the number of factors and the exterior projector, regardless of the level n.
 Reconstruction. Both occurrences of the projector degree and the tensor-power
 exponent must be N .
 Fate. Molev et al. (1996, §§2.2–2.7) develops the quantum determinant. The finite-
 level version is explicit in Futorny and Ovsienko (2005, eq. (5), §5.2).

1986-676.3    The center of a truncated Yangian                                         Class A

 Kind; source. theorem; P001287-P001288
 The notes. The principal parts of H(λ) at λ = −i generate the center, with q = 1, . . . , n
 and the written range i = 0, . . . , n − 1.
 Statement.
    QN −1    For the level-n Yangian of glN over C, multiply the rational determinant
 by i=0 (λ + i)n . The result is a monic degree-nN polynomial whose nN nonleading
 coefficients are algebraically independent generators of the center. Equivalently,
 the nN principal-part coefficients at 0, −1, . . . , −(N − 1), of orders 1, . . . , n, generate
 freely. The notebook's range of pole locations must use N − 1.
 Reconstruction. The partial-fraction and polynomial presentations are distin-
 guished, and matrix size is restored in the pole range.
 Fate. The finite-level center theorem is stated exactly in Futorny and Ovsienko
 (2005, §5.2, after eq. (5)), crediting Cherednik (1989) and Molev. This gives an
 author-publication continuation, while the original Cherednik chapter was not di-
 rectly accessible in this run.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
 tion: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-676.4   Evaluation representations and tensor products                    Class E

 Kind; source. construction; P001288
 The notes. A representation V of glN supplies a matrix LV ; tensor products are
 formed by multiplying such matrices with spectral shifts.
 Statement. The evaluation homomorphism from the full Yangian to U (glN ), followed
 by a representation and a spectral-translation
                                     P             automorphism, gives an evaluation
 module. The coproduct ∆Tij (u) = a Tia (u)⊗Taj (u) realizes tensor products as matrix
 products. A product of finite-level matrices has degree equal to the sum of their
 levels; a fixed-level quotient is not automatically a Hopf algebra.
 Reconstruction. The shift automorphism and full-versus-truncated distinction are
 supplied.
 Fate. The evaluation and coproduct statements are Molev et al. (1996, §1.16 and
 Theorem 1.28). The final finite-level caution prevents carrying the full Hopf struc-
 ture to the wrong quotient.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

1986-676.5   Young diagrams and the rational fusion procedure                  Class A

 Kind; source. construction; P001288-P001289
 The notes. A Young diagram supplies spectral shifts equal to row minus column; a
 product of elementary R-matrices and a Young operator produces the associated
 representation.
 Statement. For a standard tableau of a partition of r, take its contents with a fixed
 sign convention and ordered tensor products of fundamental evaluation modules
 at those contents. Fusion requires regularizing the rational products at coincident
 contents and applying a properly normalized tableau idempotent. The resulting
 subquotient realizes the corresponding polynomial glN representation when the
 number of rows is at most N . A bare unnormalized row-column symmetrizer is
 not interchangeable with every orthogonal tableau idempotent.
 Reconstruction. Tableau choice, regularization, dimension, and normalization are
 necessary additions.
 Fate. This is the line of Cherednik (1987), published in 1987; its role in Gelfand–
 Tsetlin bases and Yangian representations is specifically documented in Molev et al.
 (1996, Introduction). The original Duke text was blocked, so exact fusion normal-
 izations are not certified from it.
 Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
 result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 partial; verification: indirect; development: none; confidence: medium

1986-676.6   Symmetric-group normalization in the Young                        Class E
operator
 Kind; source. remark; P001289
 The notes. The order of the symmetric group is written as n in a normalization
 discussion.
 Statement. The symmetric group on n letters has order n!. Averaging over a finite
 group uses its actual order; Young symmetrizers also require their own hook-length
 normalization to become idempotents. Replacing every such normalization merely
 by n! would therefore overcorrect the passage.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  35

  Reconstruction. Only the group order is emended; the particular projector normal-
  ization remains convention dependent.
  Fate. The tensor and tableau context is the fusion construction of Cherednik (1987).
  The group-order slip is elementary and visible in the manuscript; no new theorem
  is claimed.

1.11     G. I. Olshanskii, 1987-05-05: A strong-limit
         enveloping algebra and its Yangian factor
record 677; source pages P001288-P001292.
   The talk occupies P001290 and the beginning of P001291. It defines an enlarge-
ment of the enveloping algebra through bounded-degree, tail-invariant sequences act-
ing on tensor representations, then identifies its central factor and Yangian factor.
The exact 1987 Doklady note was presented by Gelfand on 5 May 1986, matching the
manuscript heading and independently corroborating the date discrepancy.
1986-677.1 The strong-limit completion on tame tensor                             Class A
representations
  Kind; source. construction; P001290
  The notes. Sequences in U (o(n)) have bounded degrees, commute with a fixed tail
  orthogonal subgroup, and converge strongly in every tensor representation; se-
  quences are identified by their limits.
  Statement. Fix m. Consider uniformly bounded PBW-degree sequences in the tail
  centralizers whose actions have strong limits in every specified tame tensor repre-
  sentation of the infinite classical group. Quotient by equality of all these
                                                                             S represen-
  tation limits; componentwise operations give an algebra Am , and A = m Am . The
  representation family and the bounded-degree condition are part of the definition,
  not dispensable analytic decoration.
  Reconstruction. The exact tame representation class from the paper supplies the
  unspecified universal quantifier over representations.
  Fate. Olshanskii (1987, §1, Theorems 1–2) gives precisely this strong-limit construc-
  tion and an equivalent bounded-degree projective-limit construction using gln cen-
  tralizers. It was presented 5 May 1986, received 22 May 1986, published in Russian
  in 1987, and translated in 1988.
  Present status. As of November 2026, the corrected formulation is an established
  result; no unresolved claim is inferred from this occurrence.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: none; confidence: high

Yangian

  The notes. The stable algebra with m distinguished coordinates is written as a poly-

  algebra in the stable shifted power sums, and Am ≃ A0 ⊗ Y (glm ). This is the or-
