1986-710.2 Type-A Hecke algebras and braid-group                                 Class E
representations
 Kind; source. definition; P001374-P001375
 The notes. The generators satisfy (Ti + 1)(Ti − q) = 0, the adjacent braid relation and
 distant commutativity; inverse braid generators are sent to (Ti + 1 − q)/q.
 Statement. For q 6= 0, the type-A Hecke algebra Hn (q) is generated by T1 , . . . , Tn−1
 with these relations. They make Ti invertible, with Ti−1 = q −1 (Ti + 1 − q), and define
 a representation of the Artin braid group. The algebras form a compatible tower
 under the usual inclusions.
 Fate. Jones (1987, Sections 3–4) uses exactly this presentation. The theory and
 its link-invariant application were already circulating by the June 1985 Bourbaki
 lecture Connes (1986); the later 1987 article is the detailed source, not the first
 appearance.

1986-710.4    Links as braid closures                                             Class E
 Kind; source. construction; P001375
 The notes. A link is called an immersed compact oriented one-manifold in three-
 space, and closing a braid gives a link.
 Statement. A tame oriented link is an embedded finite disjoint union of circles in
 oriented three-space, considered up to ambient isotopy. Closing an oriented braid
 gives such a link, and Alexander’s theorem represents every oriented link by a braid
 closure. General immersions with double points are singular links and require dif-
 ferent equivalence data.
 Fate. Jones (1987, Section 2) states the braid-closure setting. The manuscript’s
 “immersed must be strengthened to “embedded for the knot invariant discussed
 here.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
 the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
 tion: read the result; development: annotated; confidence: high

1986-710.5    Markov equivalence of braid representatives                         Class E
 Kind; source. theorem; P001375
 The notes. Conjugation inside Bn and stabilization b 7→ bσn±1 are said to generate
 equivalence of closures.
 Statement. Two braids have ambient-isotopic oriented closures exactly when they
 are related by a finite sequence of conjugations, positive or negative stabilizations,
 and inverse destabilizations, allowing the braid index to change. Therefore a com-
 patible trace alone handles conjugation, while stabilization needs an additional nor-
 malization.
 Fate. Jones (1987, Section 2) invokes Markov’s theorem as the topological founda-
 tion of the trace construction. This was established before the lecture; the notebook
 correctly identifies the difficult converse, not just the easy invariance of individual
 moves.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
 the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct; publication match: exact; verification: read
 the result; development: annotated; confidence: high

1986-710.6    The algebraic Ocneanu trace has a second parameter                  Class E
 Kind; source. theorem; P001375
 The notes. For every q, the notebook asserts a unique factor representation with
 trace, whose values are Laurent polynomials in q.
 Statement. For fixed Hecke parameter q, the compatible algebraic trace is charac-
 terized only after a second parameter z is supplied: tr(1) = 1, tr(ab) = tr(ba), and
 trn+1 (aTn ) = z trn (a). This is an algebraic Markov trace, not automatically a positive
 state or a unique von Neumann factor representation. Values on braid words also
 involve the inverse parameter q −1 .
 Fate. Jones (1987, Theorem 5.1) proves precisely the trace theorem and explicitly
 makes z independent of q. The earlier exposition Connes (1986) explains the rela-
 tion to subfactors without asserting the notebook’s universal factor uniqueness.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
 the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-

1986-710.7    From Markov traces to two-variable link polynomials                Class E

  Kind; source. construction; P001375-P001376
  The notes. The desired function of braids is invariant under both Markov moves and
  becomes a Laurent-polynomial knot invariant.
  Statement. The normalized trace expression includes factors depending on braid
  index and exponent sum so that both stabilization signs act trivially. The general
  Hecke trace gives the two-variable HOMFLY-type invariant; the Jones polynomial
  is a particular specialization/quotient. Equality of these invariants is a necessary
  condition for isotopy, not a complete classifier of links.
  Fate. Jones (1987, Section 6) gives the normalized construction and skein relation,
  tracing the two-variable discovery to the 1985 work of Freyd, Yetter, Hoste, Lick-
  orish, Millett, Ocneanu and Przytycki–Traczyk. Connes (1986) shows that this was
  established before Kirillov’s lecture.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
  the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
  exact; verification: read the result; development: annotated; confidence: high

1986-710.8    Specialization at plus and minus one                               Class E

  Kind; source. question; P001376
  The notes. The exercise asks for the invariant obtained by setting q = ±1, mentioning
  the ratio of Jones-polynomial values.
  Statement. After choosing the usual Jones variable t and normalization Vunknot (t) = 1,
  a knot satisfies VK (1) = 1 and |VK (−1)| = |∆K (−1)|, its determinant. The Hecke pa-
  rameter q, trace parameter and link-polynomial variable must first be related; with-
  out that choice the raw exercise does not determine one numerical specialization.
  Reconstruction. The Jones normalization and the distinction between its variable
  and the Hecke parameter are supplied editorially.
  Fate. Jones (1987, Section 12, special values) gives the specialization results. They
  are established identities rather than an open question. The statement here is an
  explicit modern normalization of the exercise, not an assertion that the notebook’s
  variables were already fixed this way.

1986-711.1 Integral Chern classes and the holomorphic Hodge                       Class E
filtration
   Kind; source. theorem; P001376
   The notes. For a holomorphic bundle on a compact complex analytic manifold,
   Chern classes are integral and lie in the indicated Hodge-filtration term; cohomol-
   ogy is computed by the holomorphic de Rham complex.
   Statement. For a complex manifold X and holomorphic bundle E, ci (E) ∈ H 2i (X, Z)
   has complex image in F i H 2i (X, C). Here F i means the image of hypercohomology
   of the truncated holomorphic de Rham complex, not automatically an injectively
   embedded hypercohomology group. A Hodge direct-sum decomposition requires
   additional hypotheses, such as compact Kähler.
   Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Sections 1–2 and 8) gives the Deligne-cohomology
   setting and the compatible Chern classes; the MPI 87-2 preprint is explicitly cited
   in the following lecture. The underlying integral and filtration properties are estab-
   lished antecedents, not new conclusions of the 1987 announced work.

1986-711.3 An explicit cochain comparison with integral                         Class A
characteristic classes
  Kind; source. problem; P001377
  The notes. The problem is to construct explicitly a homotopy between the
  differential-form characteristic cocycle and an integral characteristic cocycle. The
  lecture announces Beilinson, MacPherson and Schechtman’s Notes on motivic
  cohomology.
  Statement. The comparison asks for cochains witnessing equality of the complex im-
  age of an integral Chern cocycle and the chosen filtered de Rham representative.
  A Deligne Chern class packages precisely the integral cocycle, filtered representa-

  stronger construction than the abstract existence of this lift.

  not asserted. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Proposition 8.2) gives existence and func-
  torial uniqueness, while Goncharov (2016, Section 1.7 and Section 4) provides an
  inspected explicit second-Chern construction. The final lecture records its weight-
  two ingredients.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, Deligne Chern classes and explicit low-

1986-711.4 The first Chern class from logarithms of transition                      Class E
functions
  Kind; source. construction; P001377
  The notes. For c1 , the notes use d log det gij and propose dropping the exterior deriva-
  tive to recover an integral discrepancy.
  Statement. On a sufficiently fine cover, choose holomorphic logarithms `ij of the
  determinant transitions. On triple overlaps, `ij + `jk + `ki ∈ 2πiZ, with signs adjusted
  to the chosen Cech convention. Dividing that discrepancy by 2πi gives the integral
  first Chern cocycle, and d`ij its de Rham comparison. Changing logarithm branches
  changes the cocycle by a coboundary.
  Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, weight-one complex and Chern classes) identifies
  this construction with the exponential sequence. It is the classical complete model
  for the higher comparison question, and recurs in record 712.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
  the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.

1986-712.2 The analytic Deligne complex and its degree                              Class E
convention
  Kind; source. definition; P001378
  The notes. The complex is Z → O → Ω1 → · · · → Ωp−1 , with the first arrow multiplying
  by (2πi)p .
  Statement. For a complex manifold X and integer p ≥ 1, let Z(p) = (2πi)p Z ⊂ C, and
  put Z(p)D = [Z(p) → OX → Ω1X → · · · → Ωp−1  X ] in cohomological degrees 0, . . . , p. Its

  open-variety version requires the corresponding logarithmic construction.
  Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Section 1.1 and Section 2) fixes these conventions
  and distinguishes analytic from algebraic Deligne–Beilinson cohomology. The note-
  book’s ordinary H notation must be read as hypercohomology when the argument
  is a complex.

1986-712.3   Weight-one and weight-two cohomology sheaves                        Class E

 Kind; source. theorem; P001378
 The notes. The exponential map identifies the weight-one complex with O∗ [−1]; for
 weight two the notes give C/Z(2) and Ω1 /dO.
 Statement. The exponential sequence gives Z(1)D ≃ OX     ∗ [−1]. In weight two, the

 cohomology sheaves are H1 = C/Z(2) and H2 = Ω1X /dOX . They are sheaves, not
 the global Deligne groups of X; the latter require hypercohomology. Locally the
 holomorphic Poincaré lemma identifies the second quotient with the image of d :
 Ω1 → Ω2 .
 Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Section 1.1 and the weight-one comparison in
 Section 2) verifies the exponential quasi-isomorphism. The weight-two assertions
 are the cohomology sheaves of the displayed three-term complex, with the note-
 book’s Tate factor retained.

 Kind; source. construction; P001379
 The notes. One map projects to the integer component; a second takes the exterior

 Statement. There is a forgetful morphism Z(p)D → Z(p) and a curvature morphism

1986-712.5 A chain-level Deligne product and commutativity up                    Class E
to homotopy
 Kind; source. construction; P001379
 The notes. The notes give ordinary multiplication when either factor is an integer,
 then x ∧ dy when the right form has top degree, and claim commutativity up to
 homotopy.
 Statement. For positive weights p, q, one standard asymmetric product of the trun-
 cated complexes is x∪y = xy when deg x = 0; it is x∧dy when deg x > 0 and deg y = q; it
 is zero otherwise. In particular a positive-degree left factor multiplied by a degree-
 zero right factor is zero in this model. This product is a chain map; its opposite is
 chain-homotopic to it, inducing graded commutativity on hypercohomology.
 Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Section 1.1 and Section 3, especially Proposi-
 tion 3.5 and Theorem 3.9) gives product conventions and their homotopies. The
 notebook combines the asymmetric top-form rule with two-sided integer multipli-
 cation and thereby violates the chain-map convention; one cannot keep all three
 literal rules simultaneously.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                144

  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
  the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
  Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: exact; verifica-
  tion: read the result; development: annotated; confidence: high

  The notes. Holomorphic bundles have classes cp (E) ∈ HD      (X, Z(p)), determined by

1986-712.7 Cech hypercohomology and the signed total                             Class E
differential
  Kind; source. construction; P001379-P001380
  The notes. The double complex is totalized using d + δ, with component relations
  linking an integral cocycle and forms on multiple overlaps.
  Statement. For Cech degree r, a standard total differential is D = δ + (−1)r d, unless
  one has already signed one of the two component differentials. On an acyclic cover
  or a suitable resolution, total cohomology computes the hypercohomology of the
  Deligne complex. The component equations must include the same signs and the
  initial Tate-lattice inclusion.
  Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, Sections 1–2) uses the complex-of-sheaves frame-
  work. The notebook’s abbreviated unsigned formula is acceptable only with an un-
  stated anticommuting convention; with the ordinary commuting Cech and de Rham
  differentials it is incorrect.

1986-712.9    A Deligne cocycle as an explicit comparison                            Class E
homotopy
 Kind; source. method; P001380-P001381
 The notes. The notes describe an integral cocycle, a filtered de Rham cocycle and
 a homotopy between them, then write a differential of a sum beginning with α0 ,
 although the preceding homotopy sum begins with α1 .
 Statement. The intermediate components form a total cochain of degree one less
 than the compared cocycles. With consistent total-complex signs, its differential
 is their difference. The degree-n integral cocycle α0 is one endpoint and cannot
 be included as an ordinary degree-n − 1 homotopy term. This is the mapping-cone
 interpretation of Deligne cohomology, not an equality between unsorted forms of
 different total degrees.
 Fate. Esnault and Viehweg (1987, cone construction in Section 2) provides the pre-
 cise framework. The notebook’s reference to Gabrielov–Gelfand–Losik points to
 earlier combinatorial characteristic-class comparisons; it is an antecedent, not an
 identification of every Deligne homotopy with one of their formulas.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
 the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
 Assessment. literal statement: false as written; publication match: partial; verifi-
 cation: read the result; development: annotated; confidence: high

1986-712.11   Motivic complexes and the regulator refinement                   Class B

 Kind; source. construction; P001382
 The notes. Chern classes should first take values in Z(p)M and then map through a
 polylogarithmic regulator to Deligne cohomology. Weight zero is Z, weight one is
 O∗ [−1], and negative effective weights are set to zero.
 Statement. For smooth algebraic varieties over a field, motivic cohomology and its
 complexes now have rigorous constructions, including cycle and transfer models.
 In weight one, Z(1) ≃ Gm [−1]; motivic Chern classes admit realization/regulator
 maps to the corresponding complex-analytic cohomology. The convention of zero
 negative weights belongs to the effective complex formulation, not to the assertion
 that negative Tate objects vanish in a stable motivic category.
 Reconstruction. The switch from arbitrary analytic manifolds to smooth algebraic
 varieties, the topology of the motivic sheaves and the effective/stable distinction
 are necessary modern specifications.
 Fate. Mazza et al. (2006, Lectures 3–4, 15 and 19) develops the established theory
 from Voevodsky’s 1999–2000 lectures, published in 2006. The explicit low-weight
 regulator is inspected in Goncharov (2016, Proposition 1.7). Existence of this theory
 does not prove every vanishing property proposed in the next notebook line.
 Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
 the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:

1986-712.12   Beilinson–Soule vanishing in nonpositive degrees                 Class C

 Kind; source. conjecture; P001382
 The notes. For positive weight p, the proposed motivic complex has cohomology
 only in degrees [1, p].
 Statement. The unresolved lower-degree assertion includes the Beilinson–Soulé
 vanishing conjecture: for every field F , p > 0, and j ≤ 0, H j (F, Q(p)) = 0. The
 notebook’s integral sheaf-amplitude formulation is stronger in its coefficient and
 sheaf details. Upper-degree bounds and the existence of motivic complexes must
 be separated from this nonpositive-degree vanishing.
 Fate. Garkusha (2023, Section 4 and Theorem 4.1) states the amplitude conjecture
 and proves equivalent criteria, not the general conjecture (2020 preprint, 2022
 online publication, 2023 journal issue). Soulé (2026) explicitly describes the con-
 jecture as still open in an interview dated 4 November 2026. Known arithmetic
 cases do not establish it for all fields.
 Present status. Open in general as of 12 November 2026, with direct near-cutoff
 confirmation from Soulé. The construction of motivic cohomology itself is estab-
 lished and must not be reported as solving this vanishing conjecture.
 Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:
 exact; verification: read the result; development: annotated; confidence: high;
 problem GP-1986-68
 Worth developing. Worth preserving with its exact coefficient and degree scope: it
 is a definite surviving part of the notebook program, but no bounded proof of the
 general vanishing assertion is attempted.

1986-712.13 Milnor K-theory and the top motivic cohomology                         Class B
sheaf
  Kind; source. definition; P001382-P001383
  The notes. The notes generate the Milnor algebra by units with the Steinberg re-
  lation {a, 1 − a} = 0, mention additivity in a product, and identify the top weight-p
  sheaf with Milnor K-theory.
  Statement. For a field F , K∗M (F ) is the tensor algebra over Z on the abelian group
  F ∗ , modulo the two-sided ideal generated by a ⊗ (1 − a), a 6= 0, 1. Symbols are multi-
  plicatively additive in each entry and the resulting algebra is graded-commutative.
  For smooth varieties the appropriate unramified sheaf is used; the field comparison
  is H p (F, Z(p)) ∼
                   = KpM (F ). Graded commutativity does not mean that every odd square
  vanishes integrally.
  Fate. Mazza et al. (2006, Lecture 5, Theorem 5.1) proves the field comparison and
  fixes the Milnor symbols. The tensor presentation already includes additivity in
  units; a free algebra on the underlying set of units without those relations would be
  the wrong object.

1986-712.14 The weight-two Bloch complex and the five-term                           Class E
relation
  Kind; source. construction; P001383
  The notes. The degree-one group is generated by functions avoiding 0, 1, ∞, modulo
  the alternating cross-ratio relation from five points; the differential is t 7→ t ∧ (1 − t).
  Statement. Over a field, let the pre-Bloch group be generated by [z], z 6= 0, 1, modulo
  the five-term cross-ratio relations. The map [z] 7→ z ∧ (1 − z) kills those relations
  and defines a two-term weight-two complex. The opposite sign convention (1 −
  z) ∧ z is equally valid if used consistently. Integral comparisons require careful
  antisymmetric-tensor and 2-torsion conventions; the exterior-square model is safest
  after inverting 2 or rationalizing.
  Fate. Goncharov (2016, Section 1.5) constructs the sheafified Bloch complex and its
  regulator. Suslin (1991, Introduction and Theorem 5.2) explains the exact integral
  K-theory relation and its torsion terms. The notebook itself already warns about
  2-torsion, which should not be dropped.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise statement above is established;
  the qualifications distinguish it from the abbreviated notebook formulation.
  Assessment. literal statement: correct with missing hypotheses; publication match:

1986-712.15      Suslin and the indecomposable K3 group                               Class E

  Kind; source. theorem; P001383
  The notes. Suslin is credited with connecting the Bloch complex to K-theory, and
  the construction is called Bloch–Suslin.
  Statement. For an infinite field F , the Bloch group obtained as the kernel of the
  weight-two symbol boundary is closely related to K3ind (F ) = coker(K3M (F ) → K3 (F )).
  After tensoring with Q, Suslin’s comparison identifies these groups. Integrally there
  is a roots-of-unity torsion term and a convention-sensitive modification; one must
  not identify the whole pre-Bloch group or all of K3 (F ) with the kernel.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                 148

  Reconstruction. The rational comparison is stated without the convention-sensitive
  integral extension. The classification records prior circulation, rather than inferring
  a post-1987 discovery from the printed date.
  Fate. Suslin (1991, Introduction, p. 217, and Theorem 5.2, p. 237) is the precise
  later publication: Russian original 1990, English issue 1991 (translation copyright
  1992). Suslin explicitly says that most results of the paper were obtained in 1982.
  The April 1987 notebook independently witnesses the prepublication circulation of
  the Bloch–K-theory connection. It is therefore treated as exposition of an already
  circulating result, while the introduction does not date every refinement of the final
  theorem separately. The theorem was read directly and specifies an infinite field,
  the indecomposable quotient and the modified roots-of-unity torsion term.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the precise field comparison is established.
  The notebook omits the distinction among pre-Bloch, Bloch and indecomposable K-
  theory groups, which the statement restores.

1986-712.16     The dilogarithmic regulator map                                  Class B

  Kind; source. construction; P001383-P001384
  The notes. The final diagram maps the weight-two motivic complex to the Deligne
  complex using logarithms and the dilogarithm.
  Statement. A chain-level regulator must include logarithmic correction terms and
  branch data so that the differential of the dilogarithmic term agrees with the sym-
  bol boundary and the five-term relation is respected in the target complex. A single
  uncorrected branch of Li2 (z) is not a globally single-valued map to the Deligne com-
  plex. The real Bloch–Wigner version gives a single-valued real regulator after the
  appropriate real realization.
  Fate. Goncharov (2016, Proposition 1.7 and its preceding formulas) supplies an ex-
  plicit weight-two morphism of complexes. The classical five-term and single-valued
  real formulas are treated in Zagier (2007). Earlier seminar occurrences 1974-342
  and 1979-477 concern dilogarithms/regulators but are not the same full motivic-
  complex conjecture.

1986-712.17 A cross-ratio cocycle for the second Chern class of a             Class A
rank-two bundle
  Kind; source. construction; P001384
  The notes. For a rank-two bundle and local sections si , the notes place one com-
  ponent on triple overlaps in units wedged with units and another on quadruple
  overlaps in the Bloch group, obtained from four projective points.
  Statement. Choose local nonzero sections in sufficiently general position so that
  all required pairwise determinants and cross-ratios are defined and invertible on
  the refined intersections. A triple of sections supplies an exterior product of de-
  terminant functions; a quadruple supplies a Bloch symbol of their cross-ratio. The
  Plücker identity identifies the symbol boundary with the Cech boundary of the triple
  component, and the five-term relation supplies the next cocycle identity. Degener-
  ate configurations require refinement or the appropriate resolution, not evaluation
  at forbidden cross-ratios.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  149

  Reconstruction. The general-position, nonvanishing and refinement conditions are
  supplied from the explicit cocycle construction.
  Fate. Goncharov (2016, Section 1.7, equations (1.34)–(1.44), and Section 4) pro-
  vides an inspected explicit construction matching the notebook’s last sketch, in-
  cluding the regulator to Deligne cohomology. The lecture announces the earlier
  Beilinson et al. (1987); its exact original formula was not inspected, so the later
  source is not presented as first discovery.
  Present status. As of 12 November 2026, the rank-two weight-two construction is

1.47      Developments
Scope and credit. The first two sections develop bounded parts of the class C prob-
lems GP-1986-33 and GP-1986-60. The last two are verification annotations to ques-
tions already answered in the literature or notebook. The markers [proved here] and
[computed here] identify proofs and computations supplied here; they do not assert
historical novelty. No finite calculation below is promoted to an all-level spectral the-
orem or a large-N result.

1.47.1    Quantum KdV: a level-two calculation and a Jordan
          obstruction
The question and its scope. At P001318 the notebook asks to quantize the KdV inte-
grals and then compute the spectrum in irreducible Virasoro representations. Read-
ing the latter as the spectrum of the quantized integrals, rather than of L0 alone, is an
editorial reconstruction based on the preceding clause. The notes specify no central
charge, highest weight, positivity, or completeness criterion. A precise modern target
fixes c, h ∈ C, the irreducible highest-weight module L(c, h), charge normalizations, and
a grade n; one seeks the joint generalized eigenspaces and nilpotent actions, includ-
ing multiplicities. An ordinary joint eigenbasis requires an additional semisimplicity
assertion.
    The construction of commuting charges and their spectral classification are differ-
ent problems. Bazhanov et al. (1996, eq. (11)) gives the normalization used below
(December 1994 preprint; 1996 publication). The opened May 2026 version of Ma-
soero and Ruzza (2026, Definitions 6.5–6.6 and Theorem 6.8) proves a completeness
statement at c = −2, h = (β 2 −1)/8, β ∈
                                       / Z, for the specified admissible QQ solutions mod-
ulo their entire-function gauge. This is not a theorem for every c, h. The calculation
below does not depend on that preprint.

Conventions.       Let M (c, h) be the Verma module generated by v, where
                                                                 c
         Lm v = 0 (m > 0), L0 v = hv, [Lm , Ln ] = (m − n)Lm+n + (m3 − m)δm+n,0 .
                                                                12
Its grade-two PBW basis is v1 = L−2 v, v2 = L2−1 v. Set
                                X                      c+2      c(5c + 22)
                       I3 = 2         L−n Ln + L20 −       L0 +            .
                                                        12         2880
                                n≥1

The sum is finite on every vector of the algebraic module.

Proof. The commutators give

                           L1 v1 = 3L−1 v,        L1 v2 = (4h + 2)L−1 v,
                           L2 v1 = (4h + c/2)v, L2 v2 = 6hv.

For example, L1 L2−1 v = 2L0 L−1 v + 2L−1 L0 v = (4h + 2)L−1 v. All Ln , n > 2, annihilate this
grade. Multiplication by 2L−1 and 2L−2 gives the matrix. Its nonscalar part B has
trace 16h + c + 4 and discriminant (tr B)2 − 4 det B = (c − 4)2 + 288h. If this vanishes,
the repeated-root matrix is not scalar, since its lower-left entry is 6; hence it is not
diagonalizable.

Proposition 1.2 (A Jordan block in an irreducible quotient). [proved here] For c =
1, h = −1/32, grade two survives unchanged in L(c, h). On that grade,
                                                      
                                  28893      −3/2 −3/8
                             I3 =       1+               .
                                  5120        6   3/2

The second matrix N is nonzero and satisfies N 2 = 0. Thus there is no ordinary simul-
taneous eigenbasis for the local charges on this irreducible module.

Proof. Substitution gives the displayed matrix and N 2 = 0. Every submodule is graded:
it is invariant under the diagonal operator L0 , and each vector has only finitely many
weight components. A proper submodule meeting grade two would therefore have
a nonzero singular vector in grade one or two. In grade one the condition is 2h = 0,
which fails. In grade two, annihilation by L1 , L2 requires
                                                 
                                    3     4h + 2    a
                                                       = 0.
                                 4h + c/2   6h      b

At the stated parameters its determinant is −81/64 6= 0. There is consequently no
such singular vector, and no proper submodule meets grade two. The Jordan block
persists in the irreducible quotient. A simultaneous eigenbasis would diagonalize I3 ,
a contradiction.

Interpretation and checks. This example is nonunitary: the highest-weight con-
travariant form is negative on L−1 v. It does not challenge finite-grade diagonalizability
with a positive-definite unitary inner product. It also does not refute a notebook con-
jecture: the notebook asks for a spectrum, without asserting semisimplicity.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                                        151

   The independent program code/root/check_quantum_kdv.py constructs the action by
recursive PBW rewriting, without entering the proposed matrix. A separate Gram
calculation gives
                                       
                    4h + c/2     6h
             G=                           , det G = 2h{16h2 + (2c − 10)h + c}.
                       6h    4h(2h + 1)

It checks GI3 = I3T G; at the exceptional parameters det G = −81/1024. The program also
checks Gram symmetry in grades one, three, and four at two other rational parameter
choices. All exact checks passed; the output is supplied.

Remaining obstruction. This calculation does not organize arbitrary grades, settle
every singular-vector quotient, or prove ODE/IM completeness. The reviewed sources
establish particular constructions and regimes, without documenting a reason that the
general problem was abandoned. A further bounded run could construct the charge
algebra in grades three through six and separate its Jordan loci from degeneration of
the Verma form. It would give a finite classification, not an all-grade theorem.

1.47.2    The multi-source unitary integral: evaluation, error control,
          and a failed extension
Supported and reconstructed formulations. On P001330, Kazakov asks for the in-
tegral with two Rdiagonal matrix pairs, writing identical pairs first. We use probability

Literature and the remaining target. The N = 2 reduction and the failure of an
immediate localization extension to N > 2 are treated in Holguin et al. (2024, Sec-
tions 2.2–2.3 and Appendix A) (July 2023 preprint; 2024 publication). Their displayed
angular measure and our probability Haar measure must be compared after fixing
the overall normalization. All-rank polynomial Haar moments are given by Collins
and Śniady (2006, Corollary 2.4) (February 2004 preprint; 2006 publication). Thus
an assertion that the integral has never been evaluated would be wrong if evaluation
includes a convergent explicit series. A meaningful remaining target is a tractable
formula or asymptotic description for general multi-source data in a stated regime.

                                                                                      2

The code differentiates all eighteen curves to verify the quadratic factors. Summing

CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                       154

Verification and practical limits. The program check_unitary_integral.py com-
pares the exact Gram calculation with the U (2) integral for real, complex, and co-
incident endpoints and with the ordinary rank-one HCIZ determinant at N = 3. It
tests m > N : the Gram ranks are 5 for (N, m) = (2, 3) and 14 for (2, 4). All comparisons
pass. For A = ( 13 25 ), centering at 11/2 leaves radius 1/2. At order 20 the computed
error is about 2.26 · 10−27 , below the analytic bound 3.77 · 10−24 . These decimals illus-
trate the proved bound; they are not an interval-arithmetic certificate. The separate
check_stationary_formula.py checks the counterexample by both the quadratic varia-
tions and exact moments.
   The procedure is convergent but factorial in degree. It does not supply an efficient
large-N algorithm or a compact generic formula. A further bounded run could exploit
permutation symmetries, compute a prescribed set of N = 3 moments, and test a pre-
cisely stated approximation with rigorous tails. The later literature explicitly revisits
the problem, so there is no basis to call it abandoned.

1.47.3    An annotation on simultaneous complex structures
Source and later result. Gelfand’s question at P001276 asks for a complex structure
making all the real codimension-two subspaces complex. The notebook already warns
that even-dimensional intersections are insufficient. Here a complex structure means
a single linear J ∈ EndR (R4 ) with J 2 = −1. Nonlinear equivalence of complements is a
different question. Batzies (2007, Theorems 1 and 3) gives the four-plane classifica-
tion and the transverse normal form (received 2001, revised 2005, published 2007).
The following is an elementary rederivation, not a new classification.
Proposition 1.6 (A finite-family criterion in the transverse case). [proved here] Let
P1 , . . . , Pk , k ≥ 3, be real two-dimensional linear subspaces of R4 , with Pi ∩ Pj = {0} for
i 6= j. There are real coordinates with
           P1 = R2 ⊕ 0,   P 2 = 0 ⊕ R2 ,   P3 = graph(1),       Pj = graph(Aj ) (j ≥ 4).
A common linear complex structure exists exactly when either every Aj is scalar, or a
nonscalar A among them satisfies
                      (tr A)2 − 4 det A < 0,     [A, Aj ] = 0    for every j.
For k = 4, the condition is that A4 is scalar or has negative discriminant.
Proof. In P1 ⊕ P2 , transversality makes P3 the graph of an isomorphism T : P1 → P2 . A
coordinate change in P2 makes T = 1; every other plane is then a graph. The hypothe-
ses imply det Aj 6= 0, det(Aj − 1) 6= 0, and det(Ai − Aj ) 6= 0 for distinct graph planes.
   A linear J preserving P1 , P2 is block diagonal. Preserving graph(1) makes its blocks
equal: J = diag(B, B). The remaining conditions are B 2 = −1 and BAj = Aj B. In a
basis e, Be, the centralizer of B consists exactly of a 1 + bB. A nonscalar such matrix
has discriminant −4b2 < 0, and all such matrices commute. This proves necessity.
Conversely, if every Aj is scalar, any complex structure B works. Otherwise, for a
chosen A with discriminant ∆ < 0, Cayley–Hamilton gives
                                     2A − (tr A)1
                               B=       √         ,     B 2 = −1.
                                          −∆
Every matrix commuting with A commutes with B. Thus diag(B, B) has all the required
invariances.
Corollary 1.7 (The intersection lattice is insufficient). [proved here] Arrangements of
four pairwise transverse two-planes in R4 can have the same intersection lattice, and
be joined by a transverse continuous deformation, while only one admits a common
linear complex structure.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                   155

Proof. Keep the first three planes in the normal form and use At = diag(2, 2 + t), 0 ≤
t ≤ 1. The determinants 2(2 + t) and 1 + t of At and At − 1 stay positive. All pairwise
intersections stay zero and the intersection lattice stays the rank-two uniform lattice.
At t = 0 the matrix is scalar; at t > 0 its discriminant is t2 > 0. Apply the proposition.

Independent check and boundary of the result. The code check_complex_
structures.py independently solves the full four-by-four invariance equations. It
checks scalar, nonreal, distinct-real, and Jordan cases and commuting and non-
commuting families. In the last case, the invariant algebra is only the real scalars,
excluding J 2 = −1 directly. All checks agree. This settles the stated transverse
four-dimensional linear subcase; it is not a combinatorial characterization of arbitrary
higher-dimensional or nonlinear configurations. No unspecified extension is labelled
an open conjecture here.

1.47.4    An exact audit of the cube’s 96 regions
The recorded answer. The Gelfand–T. Alekseevskaya question at P001364, item
1986-706.3, already has the written answer 96. We count the connected components
of the interior of a cube after removing every distinct affine plane determined by
three vertices. This is verification of the recorded answer, not a new problem.

Proposition 1.8 (The cube region count). [computed here] For [−1, 1]3 , the 56 vertex
triples determine 20 distinct planes. Six are faces and do not cut the interior. The
other 14 planes cut the interior into exactly 96 regions.

Proof. The cross-product calculation in check_cube_regions.py enumerates and dedu-
plicates the planes. The interior-cutting ones are

            x = ±y,   x = ±z,   y = ±z,    ε 1 x + ε2 y + ε3 z = 1   (εi ∈ {−1, 1}).

The last eight equations describe distinct planes. The code computes every triple
intersection of the full 20-plane family in the closed cube and includes its vertices,
giving 35 candidate points. Every vertex of every intermediate cell belongs to this
set: a vertex of a full-dimensional bounded three-dimensional polyhedron lies on at
least three independent bounding planes.
   Process the 14 cutting planes. A convex cell splits exactly when its candidate points
have both a strict positive and a strict negative value of the new plane equation. Linear
functions attain their extrema at vertices, so this is necessary and sufficient. Points
on the plane are kept in both new closed cells. Exact rational arithmetic gives the
successive counts
                          1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 15, 18, 26, 36, 46, 56, 72, 84, 96.
The supplied cube_witnesses.json gives a rational interior point of each final cell. All
96 sign vectors are distinct, with no zero coordinate. The cells are convex and hence
connected.

Independent count. The second calculation uses the intersection poset rather than
cell splitting. For an arrangement restricted to an open convex set, the region count
is the sum of |µ| over flats meeting that set. The usual formula is stated in Greene and
Zaslavsky (1983, Theorem A, p. 100). The restriction follows by inclusion–exclusion
with compactly supported Euler characteristic: each nonempty flat–cube intersection
is open convex in its affine span, contributing (−1)dim ; each region contributes (−1)3 .
Grouping identical intersections gives the Möbius sum, with its alternating codimen-
sion signs.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                 156

  Exact interval tests find 43 line flats meeting the cube interior, with total |µ| = 47,
and 21 point flats with total |µ| = 34. Thus the second count is

                                  1 + 14 + 47 + 34 = 96.

Flats only meeting the boundary are excluded. Both independent counts agree with
the notebook; no correction to its answer is needed.


1.48     Problem index
Earlier-year occurrence identifiers are retained as text; current-year identifiers link
to their entries.

1.48.1    GP-1982-40: Multiplicativity of regularized determinants
For specified admissible elliptic operators and a fixed regularization, determine the
defect log det(AB) − log det A − log det B, and conditions under which it vanishes. The
operator order, spectral cuts, commutativity, and treatment of kernels must be fixed.
Occurrences: 1982-587.7, 1986-668.8.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, the multiplicative anomaly and its local-
residue description are established in the settings treated by Kontsevich and Vishik
(1994). Unqualified multiplicativity is false; this occurrence reuses the earlier precise
problem.

1.48.2    GP-1986-01: Odd-moduli integration and the higher-genus
          RNS measure
For super Riemann surfaces of a fixed genus and spin parity, determine the odd-moduli
pushforward of the RNS measure on any specified projection domain, its modular
transformation, and its degeneration behavior. Separately determine whether a global
holomorphic projection to reduced spin moduli exists.
Occurrences: 1986-670.6, 1986-670.7.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, the global-projection premise is ob-
structed for g ≥ 5 by Donagi–Witten and for both parity components in every g ≥ 3 by
the theorem stated in the September 2026 Correa–Donagi–Noja preprint. Local odd
integration remains meaningful. The 2025 genus-three analysis proves a three-term
form but leaves its exact coefficient values conjectural and its numerical extraction
without a rigorous error bound. Thus that coefficient determination remains a
precise partial remainder; no general unsolved global projection is asserted.

1.48.3    GP-1986-02: Which lattice theta sums have the required
          modular transformation?
For a positive-definite lattice, determine the modular transformation of its theta se-
ries, specifying the modular group and scalar, multiplier, or vector-valued represen-
tation. The seminar asks whether the desired full-group scalar transformation forces
even unimodularity.
Occurrences: 1986-671.7.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, Poisson summation gives the precise dual-
lattice transformation, and even unimodular lattices supply the full modular-group
scalar form in the stated convention. Theta series of other integral lattices remain
modular for suitable subgroups or vector-valued representations, so the unrestricted
only-if assertion is false. This is a settled clarification, not a new open problem.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  157

1.48.4    GP-1986-03: A common linear complex structure for real
          codimension-two arrangements
Given real codimension-two linear subspaces Li ⊂ R2m , decide whether there is a
real linear operator J with J 2 = −I and J(Li ) = Li for every i. A 2-arrangement also
imposes even-codimension intersection conditions; these are additional hypotheses,
not an automatic consequence of the individual codimensions.
Occurrences: 1986-672.8.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, the proposed automatic complexification
is false. For pairwise transverse planes in R4 , Batzies (2007, Theorems 1 and 3) gives
simultaneous-conjugacy and four-plane criteria. The chapter rederives a bounded
linear-algebra formulation as an annotation to known work, with no new research
claim and no inference that a higher-dimensional classification is an open problem.
Topological equivalence is not settled by the linear criterion.

1.48.5    GP-1986-04: Polynomial quotients of the affine type-A Hecke
          algebra
For q 6= 0, identify the universal quotient of the affine type-A Hecke algebra by p(Xn ) =
0, where p is monic of degree r with nonzero constant term, and determine its dimen-
sion and a basis; the notebook singles out p(z) = z 2 − 1.
Occurrences: 1986-681.6.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, this is the cyclotomic Hecke algebra of
type G(r, 1, n), of dimension rn n!; r = 2 yields type B. The Ariki–Koike basis theorem
holds without semisimplicity, as read in Mathas (2004, §2, (2.2)). The universal quo-
tient target answers the precise notebook reading adopted here; it is not a claim of
an evaluation into the original finite type-A algebra for every polynomial.

1.48.6    GP-1986-30: Faithfulness of universal Burau representations
Determine the ranks n for which the Burau representation over Z[t, t−1 ] is faithful.
Occurrences: 1986-686.4.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, faithful for n ≤ 3, unfaithful for n ≥ 5. A
July 2026 preprint, revised September 2026, claims faithfulness for n = 4; its main
theorem was read but not independently checked here.

1.48.7    GP-1986-31: Three- and four-dimensional reduced planar
          gauge models
For a specified one-site unitary plaquette model, determine its nonperturbative planar
free energy and Wilson loops, with the center-symmetry conditions needed for volume
reduction made explicit.
Occurrences: 1986-688.7, 1986-695.14.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 the general all-coupling planar solution
is unavailable in the sources checked; exact two-dimensional reductions and higher-
dimensional numerical methods are established. Unconditional equivalence of the
original untwisted model with four-dimensional weak-coupling gauge theory is false.

1.48.8    GP-1986-32: Commuting quantizations of local KdV
          Hamiltonians
Construct commuting normally ordered deformations of the complete family of clas-
sical local KdV integrals.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                 158

Occurrences: 1986-693.6.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 solved as an algebraic/formal and generic-
parameter construction; spectral classification and exceptional representation spe-
cializations are distinct questions.

1.48.9    GP-1986-33: Quantum KdV spectrum in irreducible Virasoro
          representations
For fixed central charge and highest weight, determine the joint spectral and Jordan
data of the commuting quantum KdV charges on the irreducible highest-weight mod-
ule.
Occurrences: 1986-693.15.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 partly solved, with explicit constructions
and special-regime results. The opened 2026 Masoero–Ruzza v1 proves a particu-
lar c=-2 admissible-QQ completeness statement for nonintegral momentum; it is not
an arbitrary-parameter theorem. The chapter provides a checked level-two Jordan
obstruction in a nonunitary irreducible quotient.

1.48.10 GP-1986-34: Quantization of second Adler–Gelfand–Dickey
        structures
Construct a quantum algebra deforming the second Gelfand–Dickey bracket on monic
scalar operators of arbitrary order, in the principal Drinfeld–Sokolov setting.
Occurrences: 1986-693.16.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 the quantum W-algebra/BRST construction
resolves the algebraic problem, with precise generic-parameter identifications in the
cited sources.

1.48.11 GP-1986-35: Finite equational basis for subspace-lattice
        identities
Do identities valid in subspace lattices of all finite-dimensional rational vector spaces
follow from finitely many lattice identities? This is the explicit equational reading of
the notebook question about linear equivalence.
Occurrences: 1986-696.6.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 the stated rational formulation and the
analogous all-field variety have a negative answer by Herrmann (1984), Theorem 1.4.
The field and type of axioms were not stated in the notebook; the decidability problem
GP-1985-20 is different.

1.48.12 GP-1986-36: Preprojective reduction of the free modular
        lattice
Describe the quotient of Dr by equality in all indecomposable preprojective rational
representations and characterize its represented subspace families.
Occurrences: 1986-696.11.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026 the 1987 Gelfand–Lidskii–Ponomarev theo-
rem gives a precise large-rank answer through compatible eventually generated fam-
ilies and graded right ideals. The present chapter uses the safely verified subrange
r>=7; it does not turn the historical r=5 exception into a present-day open claim.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  159

1.48.13 GP-1986-60: The two-source unitary orbital integral
           R        P
Evaluate U (N ) exp 2µ=1 Tr(U ∗ Pµ U Qµ ) dU for diagonal source matrices, specifying
whether the target is a convergent series, a compact finite formula or large-N
information.
Occurrences: 1986-688.5, 1986-695.11, 1986-698.1.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, the general integral has exact convergent
Haar expansions; an explicit rank-two formula and rank-three expansions appear in
Holguin et al. (2024). The chapter develops a checked finite-rank formula and con-
trolled series. No general compact HCIZ-type determinant or general large-N solution
is claimed.

1.48.14 GP-1986-61: Riemann hypothesis in the toroidal and
        horocycle formulations
Do all nontrivial zeros of ζ(s) satisfy Re s = 1/2? In the recorded approach, can toroidal
spectral parameters be put in a suitable positive unitary realization, or equivalently
can the requisite RH-strength horocycle remainders be proved under the stated test-
function hypotheses?
Occurrences: 1986-700.15, 1986-700.17, 1986-703.8.
Status (2026-11): Open as of 12 November 2026. The toroidal structure theorems
and qualitative horocycle equidistribution are established, but the positivity and zero-
location conclusions are not.

1.48.15 GP-1986-62: Orientable gluings of a labelled polygon by
        genus
For a labelled oriented 2n-gon, count orientation-reversing pairings of its sides whose
quotient has genus g, with cyclic rotations kept distinct.
Occurrences: 1986-700.2, 1986-702.1, 1986-702.7.
Status (2026-11): Solved before the recorded discussions by Harer and Zagier (1986),
with an exact coefficient formula, a recurrence, and a Gaussian-matrix derivation. As
of 12 November 2026 this is an established enumeration theorem.

1.48.16 GP-1986-63: Coloured planar maps with controlled face
        counts and interfaces
Enumerate rooted orientable planar maps with specified numbers of faces of the pre-
scribed degrees and colours, and a specified number of edges between unlike colours;
specify primal/dual and rooting conventions before extracting coefficients.
Occurrences: 1986-701.1, 1986-702.8.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, formal multi-matrix generating functions
and the planar Ising solution provide the recorded framework. The notebook does
not select a unique finer counting convention, so no unsolved numerical problem is
inferred.

1.48.17 GP-1986-64: Artin holomorphy for nontrivial irreducible
        complex representations
For every nontrivial irreducible continuous finite-dimensional complex representation
ρ of the absolute Galois group of a number field, is its Artin L-function entire?
Occurrences: 1986-703.12.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                                  160

Status (2026-11): Open in general as of 12 November 2026; Brauer induction proves
meromorphic continuation, and abelian, monomial and numerous other cases are
known. Recent partial holomorphy criteria do not establish the universal conjecture.

1.48.18 GP-1986-65: The normalized mean-field spin-glass ground
        energy
                                                             P
Determine the deterministic limit of N −1 minσ∈{−1,1}N N −1/2 i<j Jij σi σj for independent
standard Gaussian couplings.
Occurrences: 1986-708.2.
Status (2026-11): Solved in the variational sense as of 12 November 2026 by the
zero-temperature Parisi formula of Auffinger and Chen (2016 preprint; 2017 publica-
tion). This is a mean-field theorem, with the normalization and Gaussian assumptions
explicit.

1.48.19 GP-1986-66: Parisi ultrametricity of asymptotic spin-glass
        overlaps
Identify a rigorous thermodynamic interpretation of the hierarchical overlap assertion
and prove ultrametricity of the resulting replica array.
Occurrences: 1986-708.4.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, Panchenko’s theorem proves ultrametric-
ity for random overlap measures satisfying the Ghirlanda–Guerra identities (2011
preprint; 2013 publication). It does not assert ultrametricity for arbitrary finite near-
ground-state sets.

1.48.20 GP-1986-67: Explicit integral and de Rham comparison for
        Chern classes
Construct explicit Cech/de Rham comparison data, preferably through low-weight mo-
tivic complexes, for the integral Chern classes of a holomorphic vector bundle.
Occurrences: 1986-711.3, 1986-712.17.
Status (2026-11): As of 12 November 2026, functorial Deligne Chern classes exist, and
inspected explicit weight-two formulas construct the comparison through the Bloch
complex and dilogarithm. The announced 1987 paper is bibliographically identified,
but its complete higher-weight formulas were not inspected. This access limitation is
not classified as mathematical openness.

1.48.21 GP-1986-68: Beilinson–Soule motivic vanishing
For every field F , positive weight p, and j ≤ 0, prove H j (F, Q(p)) = 0; compare with the
stronger integral sheaf-amplitude requirement [1, p] stated in the notebook.
Occurrences: 1986-712.12.
Status (2026-11): Open in general as of 12 November 2026. Modern motivic com-
plexes exist, the upper-degree comparison with Milnor K-theory is established, and
important arithmetic cases are known. Garkusha’s criteria and Soulé’s 4 November
2026 interview explicitly retain the general lower-degree conjecture.

Transcription reads. catalogue spans overlap; record 703 stops at P001356
Critical reading. Attribute the explicit Beilinson heading and continuation through
P001358 to 703, and the preceding Gelfand proposals to 704.
Evidence. Original scan P001355 clearly separates the two named speakers. Cata-
logue metadata are retained in talks.json.

P001356 (both; faithful error): p-adic Gaussian analogue
Transcription reads. Z_p minus zero called an open subgroup
Critical reading. It is an open multiplicative submonoid of Q_p^*; the open compact
subgroup is Z_p^*. The zeta test function is 1_(Z_p).
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                               175

Evidence. The manuscript writes Z_p without a unit star; negative valuations show
failure of closure under inversion.

P001356 (both; ambiguity): regularized power sum
Transcription reads. evaluate the first regular term at t=1
Critical reading. Specify the parameter t=e^(-u) and take the constant term in u, or
declare another regularization.
Evidence. A Laurent finite part changes under nonlinear change of parameter; ordi-
nary substitution diverges.

P001356 (both; faithful error): p-adic zeta measure
Transcription reads. one Z_p-valued measure without regularizing factor
Critical reading. Use a pseudomeasure for the zeta branch or a bounded Mazur mea-
sure with its auxiliary factor and character conventions.
Evidence. Gelbart et al. equations (2.1), (2.12), (2.13) explicitly distinguish bounded
measures from Bernoulli distributions.

P001356 (both; faithful error): unit decomposition
Transcription reads. Z_p^*=(1+pZ_p) times roots of unity of order p-1
Critical reading. State p odd; at p=2 use +1,-1 times (1+4Z_2).
Evidence. The two-adic unit group has a separate order-two factor.

P001355 (both; ambiguity): factor type
Transcription reads. III_infinity
Critical reading. The recognizable uniqueness result concerns type III_1 with separa-
ble predual; retain III_infinity as the recorded label.
Evidence. Original scan confirms infinity. Haagerup 1987 Section 2 proves unique-
ness for III_1, conventionally denoted R_infinity.

P001359 (both; faithful error): KdV and the Lax pair
Transcription reads. u_t+6u u_x+u_xxx=0 with the displayed positive A
Critical reading. For the displayed L and A, u_t=6u u_x+u_xxx; reverse t consistently
to obtain the first convention.
Evidence. Original scan shows the positive third-order operator; compatibility sup-
plies the sign check.

P001359 (both; faithful error): vacuum eigenfunction
Transcription reads. exp(ikx+4ik^3 t), eigenvalue k^2
Critical reading. For the displayed pair use eigenvalue -k^2 and exp(ikx-4ik^3 t).
Evidence. Direct substitution into both vacuum differential equations; original scan
inspected.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                               176

P001360 (both; faithful error): KP Lax pair
Transcription reads. coefficient 2 and no nonlocal time term
Critical reading. Use a consistent alpha coefficient and include the auxiliary term 3
alpha w with w_x=u_y.
Evidence. Original scan confirms 2, distinct from alpha; the displayed pair otherwise
forces extra restrictions.

P001362 (both; faithful error): zero curvature equivalence
Transcription reads. [L_i,L_j]=0 iff [M_i,M_j]=0
Critical reading. Include partial_i M_j-partial_j M_i, unless these cross derivatives
cancel.
Evidence. Composition of parameter-dependent differential operators.

P001362 (both; faithful error): example matrix A
Transcription reads. A=[[0,1],[1,0]] while A^2=-1
Critical reading. A=[[0,1],[-1,0]] is a consistent correction, retaining I=diag(1,-1).
Evidence. Direct scan confirms two positive ones. Two independent exact calculations
are preserved in check_spectral_operators.py and its output.

P001363 (both; ambiguity): matrix reality convention
Transcription reads. A^*=-A followed by real-eigenvalue resolvent formulas
Critical reading. Declare an anti-Hermitian contour and transformed potential, or
explicitly reconstruct the Hermitian model.
Evidence. The manuscript anti-Hermitian sign is faithful; later real-cut formulas re-
quire a convention change.

P001364 (both; transcription): cube-problem parenthesis
Transcription reads. vypisal?
Critical reading. The scan reads iziaschnaia, meaning elegant, in the parenthesis after
the attribution.
Evidence. Direct inspection of P001364; the word is a description of the problem, not
a copying instruction.

P001365 (both; faithful error): imaginary part and jump
Transcription reads. Im f=(f_+-f_-)/2; f_+^2-f_-^2=2 Im f Re f
Critical reading. Use Im f=(f_+-f_-)/(2i) and f_+^2-f_-^2=4i Im f Re f under usual
conventions.
Evidence. The two notebook equations are mutually inconsistent even if Im is rede-
fined as a half-jump.

P001366 (both; faithful error): resolvent square root
Transcription reads. sqrt((Vprime/(2lambda))^2-R)
Critical reading. With R = f 2 − (V ′ /λ)f , the radicand is
                                                 2
                                        V ′ /(2λ) + R.
Evidence. The preceding definition fixes the sign algebraically; the original evidence
block confirms minus R is in the manuscript.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                              177

P001369 (both; faithful error): ordinary conformal derivative
Transcription reads. partial_tilde=F partial with F=d z_tilde/dz
Critical reading. Use partial_tilde=F^(-1) partial, or reverse the definition of F.
Evidence. The evidence block and manuscript preserve both formulas; the ordinary
chain rule supplies the reciprocal.

P001369 (both; ambiguity): supermoduli dimension
Transcription reads. (3g-3|2g-2) without genus qualification
Critical reading. State unpunctured genus g>=2; include automorphism and punc-
ture corrections in other cases.
Evidence.     Witten equations (2.26)-(2.27) distinguish the dimension from the
automorphism-corrected formula.

P001371 (both; provenance): Schwarz versus Sinai catalogue
sequence
Transcription reads. record 707 before record 708
Critical reading. Retain catalogue record numbers and dates, while placing the Sinai
material on P001368-P001369 before the explicit Schwarz heading on P001369.
Evidence. Manuscript speaker headings determine the actual order.

P001368 (both; faithful error): ground energy limit
Transcription reads. lim min H
Critical reading. For the displayed SK scaling study lim N^(-1) min H.
Evidence. Auffinger-Chen Theorem 1 concerns energy per spin; total extremal energy
is extensive.

P001368 (both; faithful error): ground-state entropy
Transcription reads. many ground states differing in energy and positive exact-
ground-state entropy
Critical reading. Distinguish exact minimizers from near-minimizers in a specified
energy window; Gaussian SK has a unique minimizing spin-flip pair almost surely.
Evidence. Finite Gaussian nondegeneracy argument recorded with an independent
algebraic check in item 708.3.

P001371 (both; faithful error): quaternionic projective space
Transcription reads. CP^n and CP^1 for quaternionic lines
Critical reading. HP^n and HP^1.
Evidence. The evidence block confirms C is written, while the definition explicitly
uses quaternionic subspaces.

P001371 (both; faithful error): affine rigidity
Transcription reads. all transformations linear; simply connected gives the linear
space
Critical reading. Locally affine, including translations; a global identification with
H^n also requires suitable completeness.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                             178

Evidence. Sudbery Section 3 Theorem 1 includes the constant term; Salamon distin-
guishes locally affine structures.

P001372 (both; ambiguity): sphere dimension
Transcription reads. S^3 with three complex structures
Critical reading. Retain S^3 as written and flag impossibility; S^4 is a plausible
contextual conjecture, not a secure transcription correction.
Evidence. Original evidence block identifies superscript 3; odd-dimensional real tan-
gent spaces have no complex structure.

P001372 (both; faithful error): compact structure group
Transcription reads. Sp(1)Sp(n) for definition (1)
Critical reading. Sp(1)Sp(n) after imposing a compatible metric; definition (1) alone
has GL(n,H).
Evidence. Salamon Section 1 identifies the general stabilizer and its maximal compact
subgroup.

P001373 (both; faithful error): quaternion algebra in tangent
automorphisms
Transcription reads. subalgebra in Aut T_xM
Critical reading. subalgebra in End T_xM, whose nonzero quaternionic elements act
invertibly.
Evidence. Aut is a group and does not contain the zero endomorphism; the earlier
definition on P001372 correctly uses End.

P001375 (both; faithful error): link definition
Transcription reads. immersed compact oriented one-manifold in R^3
Critical reading. embedded tame compact oriented one-manifold, unless a theory of
singular links is intended.
Evidence. The manuscript evidence block confirms immersed; Alexander-Markov the-
ory in this lecture uses embeddings.

P001375 (both; faithful error): factor trace claim
Transcription reads. unique factor representation for every q
Critical reading. Unique algebraic Markov trace after fixing its additional parameter
z; positivity and factor realizations are separate questions.
Evidence. Jones 1987 Theorem 5.1 states the two-parameter trace theorem.

P001376 (both; faithful error): roots of unity
Transcription reads. Jones excludes roots of unity from consideration
Critical reading. Only specified generic semisimplicity formulas exclude them; Jones
explicitly emphasizes and studies them.
Evidence. Jones 1987 discussion following Theorem 4.5 and the special-value/root-of-
unity sections directly contradict the blanket attribution.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                              179

P001376 (both; faithful error): Hodge filtration
Transcription reads. F^j H^k equals hypercohomology of the truncated complex
Critical reading. Use the image in de Rham hypercohomology; injectivity and a Hodge
decomposition require additional conditions.
Evidence. The definition of a filtration is by this image; compactness alone does not
imply the Kahler Hodge decomposition.

P001377 (both; faithful error): trace-power characteristic class
Transcription reads. c_i(E)=Tr(A^i)
Critical reading. Use the normalized Chern-character component (1/i!)Tr(a^i), or the
elementary invariant polynomial for c_i.
Evidence. The manuscript evidence block confirms c_i; already for i=2 the elementary
symmetric polynomial differs from the power sum.

P001378 (both; ambiguity): Contemporary Mathematics reference
Transcription reads. approximately volume 25
Critical reading. Beilinson, Notes on absolute Hodge cohomology, Contemporary
Mathematics 55, Part I (1986), 35-68.
Evidence. Bibliography of the explicitly cited Esnault-Viehweg preprint identifies the
work.

P001379 (both; faithful error): Deligne product with an integer on
the right
Transcription reads. omega_1 star z_2=z_2 omega_1, together with the asymmetric
top-form rule
Critical reading. For the displayed asymmetric chain product, positive-degree x cup
degree-zero y is zero.
Evidence. Esnault-Viehweg Section 1.1 and the homotopic product models in Section
3; retaining both integer rules violates the chain-map identity.

P001380 (both; faithful error): Hodge decomposition hypothesis
Transcription reads. For compact X, H^n is the direct sum of H^(a,b)
Critical reading. Add compact Kahler, or another hypothesis giving the asserted
Hodge decomposition.
Evidence. Compact complex manifolds need not have a degenerating Hodge-to-de
Rham spectral sequence.

P001380-P001381 (both; faithful error): Deligne exact sequence
lattice
Transcription reads. ordinary Z in every weight and an intersection inside complex
cohomology
Critical reading. Use the Tate lattice Z(p), and use the preimage of F^p in integral
cohomology to retain torsion.
Evidence. Esnault-Viehweg basic exact sequences; torsion maps to zero in complex
cohomology.
CHAPTER 1. THE ACADEMIC YEAR 1986–87                                             180

P001381 (both; faithful error): comparison homotopy, equation 19
Transcription reads. d(alpha_0+alpha_1+...+alpha_i)=alpha_0-d alpha_i
Critical reading. Use the signed total differential of the degree-(n-1) cochain built
from alpha_1,...,alpha_i; alpha_0 is an endpoint.
Evidence. The manuscript itself starts the homotopy sum at alpha_1 on the previous
line; alpha_0 has a different total degree.

P001381 (both; faithful error): first Chern logarithm
Transcription reads. log(deg g_ij)
Critical reading. log(det g_ij), with the 2pi i lattice normalization.
Evidence. The evidence block confirms deg in equation 24 and det in equation 21;
only the determinant is a transition function of det E.

P001382 (both; faithful error): motivic Chern class membership
Transcription reads. c_i,alg(E)=H^(2i)(X,Z(i)_M)
Critical reading. c_i,alg(E) belongs to H^(2i)(X,Z(i)_M).
Evidence. The source evidence block confirms an equals sign; the left side is a class
and the right side a group.

P001383 (en; translation): historical name
Transcription reads. First devised by Spencer, Bloch
Critical reading. First devised by Spencer Bloch, one mathematician; the given name
is not a separate attribution.
Evidence. The manuscript name Spencer is confirmed; Bloch is Spencer Bloch, as in
the cited original K-theory literature.

P001342-P001343 (both; faithful error): toroidal spectral criterion
Transcription reads. unitarity of the representation would imply RH
Critical reading. The relevant spectral conclusion is temperedness; unitarity alone
includes complementary series.
Evidence. Cornelissen-Lorscheid Introduction states the RH strategy with tempered
constituents; the positive pairing alone is not enough.
